PREFACE 
 I, the Chairman of the Department-related Parliame ntary Standing Committee on Home 
Affairs, having been authorized by the Committee to  submit the report on its behalf, do hereby 
present this One Hundred and Ninety Seventh Report on Demands for Grants (2016-17) relating to 
the Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA). 
2.0 The Committee at its meeting held on 13 
th  April, 2016 to examine the Demands for Grants 
(2016-17) of the Ministry of Home Affairs and heard  the Home Secretary and other officers 
thereon.  
3.0 The Committee in its sitting held on 2 
nd  May, 2016 considered and adopted the Report. 
4.0 The Committee while making its observations/rec ommendations has mainly relied upon the 
following documents:- 
(i) Address by the President of India to  both Hous es of Parliament assembled  together 
on 23 rd  February, 2016; 
(ii) Speech of Finance Minister on 29 th  February, 2016 while presenting the Union 
Budget 2015-2016; 
(iii) Detailed Demands for Grants of the Ministry of Home  Affairs for the year 2016-
2017; 
(iv) Annual Report of the Ministry for the year 2015-2016; 
(v) Outcome Budget of the Ministry 2016-17; 
(vi) Detailed Explanatory Note on Demands for Grants of the Ministry of Home Affairs 
for the year 2016-2017; 
(vii) Projection of outlays for the schemes to be underta ken by the Ministry during the 
Twelfth Five Year Plan; 
(ix) Details of under-utilization of the allocations mad e under different heads during the 
last three years; 
(x) Written replies furnished by the Ministry to the Qu estionnaires sent to them by the 
Secretariat;  
(xi) Presentations made by the Home Secretary and other concerned officers; and 
(xii) Written clarifications furnished by the Minis try, on the points/issues raised by the 
Members during the deliberations of the Committee.  
5.0 For facility of reference and convenience, obse rvations and recommendations of the 
Committee have been printed in bold letters in the body of the Report.

REPORT 
CHAPTER-I  
 
OVERVIEW 
 
1.1 According to the Annual Report (2015-16) of the  Ministry of Home  Affairs, in terms of 
Entries 1 and 2 of List II -"State List' - in the S eventh Schedule to the Constitution of India  'public 
order' and 'police' are the responsibilities of Sta tes, however, Article 355 of the Constitution 
enjoins the Union to protect every State against ex ternal aggression and internal disturbance and to 
ensure that the Government of every State is carrie d on in accordance with the provisions of the 
Constitution. The Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA) ha s responsibilities for internal security, 
border management, Centre-State relations, administ ration of Union Territories, management of 
Central Armed Police Forces, disaster management, e tc.    While pursuing these obligations,  the 
Ministry of Home Affairs continuously monitors the internal security situation, issues appropriate 
advisories, shares intelligence inputs, extends man power and financial support, guidance and 
expertise to the State Governments for maintenance of security, peace and harmony without 
encroaching upon the constitutional rights of the States.  
 
GOALS AND OBJECTIVES 
 
1.2 The outcome budget (2016-17) of Ministry of Hom e Affairs throws light on goals and 
objectives of the MHA.  According to it, MHA admini sters and implements a wide arch of 
schemes and programmes. In brief, the goals and objectives of the Ministry are as follows:- 
 
/head2right Ensuring internal security of the country; 
/head2right Promoting harmonious Centre-State relations;  
/head2right Administering the Union Territories efficiently; 
/head2right Preserving and promoting national integration and communal harmony; 
/head2right Raising, administering and deploying Central Armed Police Forces (CAPFs); 
/head2right Modernizing State Police Forces; 
/head2right Protecting and upholding the principles of human rights; 
/head2right Managing effectively the international borders and coastline; 
/head2right Extending relief and mitigating the hardships on account of disasters; 
/head2right Working for the welfare of freedom fighters; 
/head2right Carrying out the population census decennially; 
/head2right Preventing and combating drug trafficking and abuse;  
/head2right Implementing the official language policy; and  
/head2right Administering the Indian Police Service Cadre. 
 
1.2.1 Outcome Budget (2016-17), MHA envisages that under the Government of India 
(Allocation of Business) Rules, 1961, the Ministry of Home Affairs has the following constituent 
Departments:- 

10 
 
/head2right Department of Internal Security (deals with police, law & order and rehabilitation); 
/head2right Department of States (deals with Centre-State relat ions, Inter-State relations, Union 
Territories and freedom fighters’ pension); 
/head2right Department of Home (deals with the notification of assumption of office by the 
President and the Vice-President, notification of a ppointment of the Prime Minister 
and other Ministers, etc); 
/head2right Department of Jammu & Kashmir Affairs (deals with t he constitutional provisions in 
respect of the State of Jammu and Kashmir and all o ther matters relating to the State 
excluding those with which the Ministry of External Affairs is concerned); 
/head2right Department of Border Management (deals with managem ent of borders, including 
coastal borders);   
/head2right Department of Official Language (deals with the imp lementation of the provisions of 
the Constitution relating to official languages and  the provisions of the Official 
Language Act, 1963 and the Official Language Rules, 1976). 
/head2right The Office of the Registrar General and Census Comm issioner of India mainly 
dealing with all matters relating to the Census ope rations inclusive of all related data 
thereto and the preparation of the National Populat ion Register (NPR) for assigning 
unique identity number to each individual.  
1.2.2 The Ministry of Home Affairs is divided into twenty (20) divisions for administrative 
purposes which are as follows:- 
1. Administration Division 
2. Border Management Division 
3. Coordination Division 
4. Centre State Division 
5. Disaster Management Division 
6. Finance Division 
7. Foreigners Division 
8. Freedom Fighters & Rehabilitation Division 
9. Internal Security - I Division 
10. Internal Security - II Division 
11. Internal Security - III Division 
12. International Co-operation Division 
13. Jammu & Kashmir Division 
14. Judicial Division 
15. Left Wing Extremism (LWE) Division 
16. North-East Division 
17. Police- I Division 
18. Police- II Division 
19. Police Modernization Division 
20. Union Territories Division 
 
1.2.3  The Department of Official Language is headed by a separate Secretary and functions 
independently. The Departments of Internal Security , Home, Jammu & Kashmir Affairs and 
Border Management, function under the Union Home Secretary and are inter-linked. 
 

11 
 
1.2.4 Delhi Police which is responsible for maintai ning law and order in National Capital 
Territory of Delhi, functions under the administrative control of Ministry of Home Affairs. 
 
1.2.5 The Ministry also has the following Central P olice Organisations functioning under its 
administrative control:- 
 
1. Assam Rifles; 
2. Border Security Force; 
3. Central Industrial Security Force; 
4. Central Reserve Police Force; 
5. Indo-Tibetan Border Police;

are as follows:-

2. National Crime Records Bureau;

CHAPTER-II  
OVERALL ASSESSMENT OF DEMANDS FOR GRANTS 
2.0 There are nine Demands for Grants which are adminis tered by Ministry of Home Affairs. 
Earlier there were ten grants; however, from this y ear the grant named "Other Expenditure of 
MHA"  (it was earlier known as Grant No. 56) has been mer ged with Grant No. 46-MHA.  The 
total allocation for nine Demands for Grants of the  Ministry of Home Affairs for the year 2016-17 
in Budget Estimates is Rs. 88545.78 Crore. In the B E 2015-16, it was Rs. 78950.35 crore which 
was increased to Rs. 80614.81 crore at RE stage. Th us, there is an increase of 9.83% fund 
allocation over RE 2015-16 and 12.15% over BE 2015- 16. The Ministry has informed that out of 
Rs. 88545.78 crore allocated in BE 2016-17; Rs. 150 75.00 crore is under Plan allocation whereas 
Rs. 73470.78 crore is earmarked under Non-Plan allocation. 
2.1 The Demands of the Ministry of Home Affairs are  divided into two volumes - Volume-I 
(Demand Nos. 46 – 49) and Volume –II (Demand Nos. 9 0 – 94).  According to the Ministry, 
Volume-I pertains to the Ministry of Home Affairs, Cabinet, Police, Other Expenditure of MHA 
and Transfer to Union Territory Governments with Le gislature. The Demand “Transfers to Union 
Territory Governments with Legislature” covers Assistance to Union Territories of Puducherry and 
Government of NCT of Delhi in the form of Grants an d Loans for gap funding. The Budget 
Provision under the Head ‘Plan’ is decided by Minis try of Finance in consultation with Planning 
Commission. Non-Plan support is extended only in re spect of UT of Puducherry to meet their 
Non-Plan deficit, as NCT of Delhi has a Non-Plan Re venue surplus. According to the Ministry, it 
releases the Grant on quarterly basis and have a li mited role in scrutinizing, sanctioning and 
implementation of the schemes/projects in respect of these UTs.  
 
2.2 Similarly, Volume-II pertains to the five Grant s (90 – 94) which relate to five Union 
Territories without legislatures namely Andaman & N icobar Islands, Chandigarh, Dadra & Nagar 
Haveli, Daman & Diu and Lakshadweep. The Ministry h as informed that though, provisions for 
the Union Territories without Legislature form part  of the Demands of the Ministry of Home 
Affairs, different programmes /schemes are the subj ect matter of the other Ministries which are 
responsible for scrutinizing, sanctioning and implementing the schemes/projects falling under their 
purview. The Administrators/Heads of these Union Te rritories without Legislature have been 
endowed with financial and administrative powers. B ased on the ceilings fixed by the Ministry of 
Finance on ‘Non-Plan’ expenditure and by Planning Commission for ‘Plan’ expenditure, the Union 
Territory Administrations prepare budget allocation s for the schemes against the 
Ministries/Departments of the Central Government wh ich are administratively concerned. This 
budgetary break-up is included in the Demands for G rants of the respective UTs and presented by 
Ministry of Home Affairs. 
 
2.3 Two out of these nine Grants namely Grant No. 4 6 and 48 are directly controlled by the 
Ministry of Home Affairs.  According to the Ministr y, Rs. 88545.78 Crore allocated to Ministry of 
Home Affairs this financial year under above discussed nine grants, has been distributed as under:-

2.6 The Committee notes that there is an increase o f 12.15% over BE 2015-16 and 9.84% 
over RE 2015-16 to the Ministry of Home Affairs BE 2016-17.    The Committee apprehends 
that this nominal increase would just meet the infl ationary pressures. The Committee 
recommends that the Ministry after making a thoroug h scrutiny of its needs to run the 

15 
 
projects in hand and projects proposed, may approac h the Ministry of Finance for 
enhancing allocation at RE stage, otherwise ongoing schemes and projects would be seriously 
affected.   Besides, currently launched projects would also be adversely affected.   
2.7 When the Ministry was asked whether the MHA/PMO /Finance Ministry has revisited or 
reviewed the issue of transferring of eight Central ly sponsored scheme of Ministry of Home 
Affairs merged into an Umbrella Centrally Sponsored  Scheme called "National Scheme for 
Modernization of Police and other forces to the States, the Ministry has replied as under:- 
"Yes, MHA has written to Secretary (Expenditure) an d Principal Secretary to PM 
stressing the need for continuation of the Umbrella  Centrally Sponsored Scheme 
called “National Scheme for Modernization of Police  and other forces” on 
24.03.2015 and 25.03.2015 respectively. As per DO l etter dated 4.11.2015 from 
Finance Secretary to Home Secretary regarding ratio nalisation of schemes, 
action has already been initiated to seek appraisal  by EFC/CNE and approval of 
the Cabinet to the proposed merger of schemes formi ng an Umbrella Scheme of 
Modernisation of Police Forces. The major Schemes w hich will form Umbrella 
Scheme are Crime and Criminal Tracking Network & Sy stem (CCTNS); Inter-
State Police Wireless; Assistance to LWE Management ; Security Related 
Expenditure (SRE) for Jammu & Kashmir, LWE Areas, N E Areas; Modernisation 
of State Police; India Reserve Battalions, etc. MHA  is proposing for inclusion of 
SIS, SRE Scheme in umbrella scheme of Police Modernization." 
2.8 Elaborating on the negative consequences of tra nsferring these central schemes to the 
states, the Ministry has submitted as under:- 
"It has been observed that the schemes proposed to be included in the umbrella 
scheme concern National Security, Law & Order and t herefore require support from 
the Central Government. The group of Chief Minister s constituted to consider the 
matter has observed that Law & Order and justice de livery system constitute core 
national development agenda and therefore continuat ion of central support to these 
schemes is critical. Special Infrastructure Scheme (SIS), was being implemented 
since 2008-09 in LWE affected States for providing central assistance to States for 
filling up critical infrastructure gaps of their special  security forces, which could not 
be covered under any other existing scheme. During the 11 th  Plan period (2008-09 to 
2011-12), Rs.445.82 crore and during 12 th  Plan period (2013-14 and 2014-15), Rs. 
122.13 crore has been released to the LWE affected States under the Scheme." 
2.9 The Ministry has further submitted that accepti ng the recommendation of the 14 th  Finance 
Commission to enhance the share of the  States in t he net proceeds of Union taxes from 32% to 42%, 
the Government has delinked the SIS from Central as sistance from 2015-16. According to the 
Ministry, the Scheme has been transferred to the St ates for giving them sufficient flexibility to 
conceive and implement scheme/programmes suited to their local needs, including requirement to 
bridge the developmental deficit gaps.  
2.10 The Ministry has admitted that the scheme was useful for training, infrastructure, residential 
infrastructure, weaponry, vehicle and any other rel ated items pertaining to the upgradation and fillin g 
of critical gaps for Special Forces of LWE affected  States and therefore, keeping in view the 
importance of the Scheme, MHA has proposed to reviv e this scheme as a component of Umbrella 

16 
 
Scheme namely, ‘Modernization of Police Forces of t he Central Government from 2016-17 in 
consultation with the Department of Expenditure.  
 
2.11 During the meeting of the Committee held on 13
th  April, 2016 while replying to  a pin 
pointed query of the Chairman of the Committee, the  Secretary in-charge of the Ministry of Home 
Affairs submitted before the Committee that  the as sistance to States through Centrally sponsored 
Umbrella scheme has been revived. 
   
2.12 The Committee is in agreement with the view su bmitted by the Ministry of Home 
Affairs that the schemes to be included in the Cent rally Sponsored umbrella Scheme called 
"National Scheme for Modernization of Police and ot her forces" are of National Security 
importance and are vital for strengthening of inter nal security architecture in the country 
and continuation of support of the Central Governme nt to these schemes is critical.  The 
Committee is hopeful that the proposal of Home Mini stry for inclusion of SIS, SRE Scheme 
in umbrella scheme will be considered as these sche mes are vital for Left Wing Extremism 
affected and other troubled regions/states of the country. 
2.13 The Committee is not ready to buy the argument  that merely by enhancing the share 
of the  States in the net proceeds of Union taxes f rom 32% to 42%, the Central Government 
can justify delinking the SIS from Central assistan ce.   Besides, the Committee is of the view 
that increasing of share of the States in the  divisible pool of Union taxes to 42 percent from 
32 percent would not yield the desired results as due to the imposition of various types of cess 
and surcharge in the  BE 2016-17 such as Krishi Kalyan cess, Education cess, Swachh Bharat 
cess, Clean Energy cess etc. which go directly to t he centre's coffer not to the States, states’ 
share in the divisible pool is likely to go down in stead of 42% as claimed.  The Committee 
accordingly proposes that the decision on modality of the implementation of the Umbrella 
scheme may be devolved to the States in terms of sufficien t flexibility to conceive and 
implement scheme/programmes suited to their local n eeds but financial support from the 
Centre should continue as States particularly backw ard states have their own social sector 
commitments. 
2.14 According to the Ministry, the major achieveme nts made by the Ministry of Home Affairs 
during the year 2015-16 were as under:- 
(i) The overall internal security situation in the coun try remained under control. Only 
one major incident of terror attack occurred in the  hinterland of the country at 
Dinanagar, Distt. Gurdaspur in 2015 which was succe ssfully foiled by the security 
forces.  
(ii) There is significant improvement in the overall sec urity situation in the hinterland, 
the North-Eastern States and Left Wing Extremism (L WE) affected States. This 
has been made possible through sustained efforts an d measures taken by the 
central security forces and agencies in collaborati on with the State Governments 
concerned. 
(iii) India and Bangladesh signed a historic agreement in  June 2015 as a result of 
which 111 Indian enclaves in Bangladesh and 51 Bang ladeshi enclaves in India 
were physically exchanged on 31 
st  July, 2015.  This has resolved a long pending 
complex issue between the two countries for their m utual benefits. The 

17 
 
Government has also sanctioned a package of Rs.1,00 5.99 crores for rehabilitation 
of the returnees and creation/up-gradation of infra structure in the enclaves 
transferred to India.  Similar improvements in infr astructure and operational 
capabilities have also been approved in respect of Indo-China border. 
(iv) Women security and increase in cyber-crime are the new challenges facing the 
country.  The Government assists the States to set up investigative units for crime 
against women utilizing the ‘Nirbhya Fund’.  The Go vernment has also recently 
extended the revamped Crime and Criminal Targeting Network System (CCTNS) 
project with an objective of fully computerising th e functioning of Police Stations, 
interlinking and sharing of data amongst police, co urts, prisons, prosecution and 
forensic laboratories.  The project will be fully i mplemented by March 2017 and 
bring in significant improvement in the criminal justice system in India. 
(v) Modernization and upgradation of immigration servic es have been included as one 
of the Mission Mode project to be undertaken under national e-governance plan. 
The e-tourist visa scheme was launched on 27 th  Nov., 2014 for 43 countries at 9 
Indian airports. The scheme has been extended to 11 3 countries at 16 Indian 
airports, it is proposed to extend the scheme to 15 0 countries by March, 2016; 
Since the Launch of scheme approx 6,50,000.00 visas  have been issued under the 
scheme. 
(vi) The country had to face severe drought and floods in some States.  The Government 
is supportive in terms of supplementation of physic al and financial resources of the 
States to handle such disaster.  An amount of Rs.16 ,441.22 crores has been released 
under the State and National disaster response fund  for management of relief 
necessitated by the natural disaster in the affected areas. 
(vii) Levels of infiltration along the Indo-Pak border in  J&K have gone down 
significantly in 2015 as compared to 2014. Besides,  the level of terrorist violence in 
J&K was also kept under control. There was also a s ignificant reduction in death of 
civilians and security forces in cross border firin g and terrorist violence against a 
significant increase in the number of militants including top commanders of militant 
outfits being neutralised. 
(viii) The Government has sanctioned (November 18, 2015) a  package of Rs. 2,000 crore 
providing for additional 3,000 State Government job s and 6,000 transit 
accommodations for Kashmiri migrants and relief to the migrants of the hilly areas 
of Jammu Division at par with the Kashmiri migrants  i.e. cash relief of Rs. 2,500 
per head per month (maximum Rs.10,000 per family pe r month) plus relief for 
ration/fodder, involving a total estimated expenditure of Rs.13.45 crore per annum. 
(ix) As part of its ease-of-doing business policy, the M inistry of Home Affairs has 
liberalized policy for National Security Clearance on 01.07.2015 for sensitive 
sectors of the economy by codifying all existing pr actices with the objective to 
evaluate potential threats, visible or embedded in proposals received by the MHA 
and to provide a national risk assessment from the point of view of threats falling 
within the above said scope. In the year 2015, a to tal number of 1,744 security 
clearances were conveyed by the MHA compared to 815 in 2014 and 712 in 2013. 
(x) A Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) has been signed  between India and 
Bangladesh on 06.06.2015, to prevent and counter sm uggling and circulation of 
fake currency notes, during the Hon’ble Prime Minister’s visit to Bangladesh 

18 
 
(xi) Decline in LWE violence continues, killing of civil ians and security forces 
decreased by 26% and 33% respectively in 2015 over the corresponding period of 
2014.  On anti-LWE front, killing of LWE cadres inc reased by 33% over the 
corresponding period of  2014.  Many top leaders arrested in 2015. 
(xii) The Procedure for Security Clearance of proposals r elating to Defence/ 
Infrastructure Projects  was finalized.  
(xiii) The Maintenance Policy for the Border Infrastructur e (i.e. Border Roads, Flood 
Lights, Border Fencing and Border Outposts) has been finalized. 
(xiv) National Database of Arms Licensees (NDAL) was created in October, 2012. Since 
May, 2014 concerted efforts were made to reach out to 720 licensing authorities 
across the States and UTs.  Due to this approximate ly  26 lakhs records could be 
uploaded  from May, 2014  till December, 2015.  
(xv) During the period from May, 2014 to Dec, 2015, Narc otics Control Bureau (NCB) 
conducted 1532 awareness programmes against drug ab use in various States/ UTs 
in the country.  
(xvi) Law & Order in the North Eastern Region has been im proving substantially in 
terms of reduction in violent incidents and lower civilian casualties. 
(xvii) In Assam, Suspension of Operation (SoO) agreements signed with National 
Democratic Front of Bodoland/Progressive [NDFB/P] a nd National Democratic 
Front of Bodoland/ Ranjan Daimary [NDFB/RD] have be en extended for a further 
period of six months up to 30.6.2016.  Besides, SoO  agreement signed between 
Karbi Longri NC Hills Liberation Front (KLNLF) and Government of Assam since 
2010 has been extended upto 30.6.2016. 
(xviii) A total of 25 UG outfits of Manipur, under two cong lomerates (United Progressive 
Front[UPF] -8, Kuki National Organisation [KNO] -17 ) are presently under 
Suspension of Operation with the Government.  Talks  with these outfits are 
continuing and SoO agreements signed with these gro ups have been further 
extended for a further period of one year upto 08.0 6.2016 and 21.7.2016 
respectively. 
(xix) In Nagaland, Ceasefire agreement signed with Nation al Socialist Council of 
Nagaland/Reformation [NSCN/R] on 28.04.2015 for a period of one year. Ceasefire 
with NSCN (Khole-Kitovi) extended for further one y ear upto 28.04.2016. MHA’s 
Interlocutor entered into a framework agreement wit h NSCN (Isak Muviah) on 
30.08.2015. 
(xx) In Order to strengthen the Victim Compensation Sche me launched by States/UTs, 
‘Central Victim Compensation Fund (CVCF)’ has been sanctioned and Guidelines 
for Operating CVCF have been circulated among the States/ UTs.   
(xxi)          MHA has initiated steps for setting up Nat ionwide emergency Response System 
(NERS) having “112” as the emergency number which c an be dialed from 
anywhere in the country by anyone to seek police as sistance.  People from any 
geographic region in the country will be able to ac cess this emergency system 
through a number of channels like landline phone, m obile phone, e-mail, SMS, 
chatting, Voice over Internet, etc., which will giv e them a range of options suitable 
to the people in different situations. Request for proposal for selection of IT Service 
provider to implement National Emergency Response s ystem (NERS) funded under 
Nirbhaya fund was issued.  DOT allocated emergency number ‘112’ for this 

19 
 
service.  Guidelines for implementing of NERS by the States/UTs have been issued. 
Government of Gujarat, Chhattisgarh and Telengana h ave signed MoU for 
implementation of NERS project. 
(xxii) Government on March 20, 2015 approved 33% reservati on for women in direct 
recruitment in Non-Gazetted posts from Constable to  Sub-Inspector in the CAPFs 
& police forces of all the UTs including Delhi Police. 
(xxiii) Home Ministry in close coordination with the State Governments took up two 
specific campaigns ‘Operation Smile’ during January  2015 and ‘Operation 
Muskaan’ in July, 2015 to rescue missing children a nd reunite them with their 
parents.  Both the campaigns resulted in rescue of more 28,000 missing children.  A 
large number of police personnel got trained and se nsitized about this important 
social problem.  All the States have been requested  to take up Operation Smile-II 
during January 2016. 
(xxiv) In order to motivate the police personnel engaged i n searching missing children, 
MHA for the first time recognized their contributio n in the National Conference 
held on 07.10.2015 on Human Trafficking.   
(xxv) Web Portal Mysecurity.gov.in was launched by HM on 7 th  October, 2015.  This 
web portal is a platform for individuals, companies , students, NGOs etc. to develop 
and deploy security applications which may be useful to people. 
(xxvi) A memorandum of Understanding between India and Ban gladesh on Bi-lateral 
Cooperation for Prevention of Human Trafficking in Women and Children, Rescue, 
Recovery, Repatriation and Re-integration of Victim s of Trafficking was signed 
during the visit of Hon’ble Prime Minister to Bangl adesh in the month of June, 
2015. 
(xxvii) Agreement on Transfer of Sentenced Persons has been  signed with Hong Kong, 
Australia, Nigeria, Qatar, Mongolia and Kazakhstan during the year. 
(xxviii) 78% (Approx 12000) Police stations are registering 100% FIR. An advisory to 
ensure no discrimination in registering FIR was issued on 12.08.2015. 
(xxix) Central Crime & Criminal database has been created.   As a result, Data from 21 
States/UTs are being replicated. 
(xxx) An Expert Group has been constituted to prepare a R oad Map for effectively 
tackling all the Cyber Crimes and give suitable rec ommendations on all facets of 
Cyber Crimes.  The committee has submitted its repo rt.  The report has been 
approved by Hon’ble Home Minister. 
(xxxi)  “Crime in India, Accidental Deaths & Suicides in I ndia” and “Prison Statistics, 
2014”were published by NCRB. 
(xxxii) First Meeting of India-Israel Joint Working Group o n Border Management and 
Security was held on 25 th  March, 2015. 
(xxxiii) To strengthen the co-ordination between the Central  and the State agencies and to 
discuss the issues relating to Coastal Security, th e meeting of the Consultative 
committee for the Ministry of Home Affairs was held  under the Chairmanship of 
Hon’ble Home Minister on 13 th  July, 2015 at Panaji, Goa. 
(xxxiv) On 2 nd  December, 2015, a Govt. of Bahrain Delegation led by LT. GEN. Rashid bin 
Abdullah al Khalifa, Hon’ble Minister of Interior m et a GoI Delegation led by 
Hon’ble Union Home Minister. Matters of mutual inte rest related to security 
cooperation were discussed. An Agreement between th e Government of the 

20 
 
Republic of India and the Government of the Kingdom  of Bahrain on Cooperation 
in Combating International Terrorism, Transnational  Organized Crime and 
Trafficking in Illicit Drugs, Narcotic and Psychotr opic Substances and Precursors 
Chemicals was signed between the two sides. 
(xxxv) With a view to instilling confidence in women, to h elp women in distress and to 
ensure their safety in Delhi, Ministry of Home Affa irs has approved launching of 
the Mobile App 'Himmat' by Delhi Police on 1 st  January, 2015.  This application 
helps women to raise SOS alert in case of any emerg ency and the same gets 
reported along with victim/ caller location in real  time at Police control room and 
the nearest PCR Van and local police can be rushed to the spot. 
(xxxvi) Layout Plan for Central Armed Police Force Institut e of Medical Science 
(CAPFIMS) has been updated and revised based on lan d availability at Maidan 
Garhi (New Delhi) and approved on 30.11.2015. Work has been assigned to CPWD 
and expenditure sanction for Rs.1,219.21 crore issued on 30.11.2015. 
(xxxvii) Centralized Visa Issuance System (C-Visa) has been implemented in 23 Indian 
Missions abroad during the period. So far, a total of 163 Indian Missions have been 
covered. Biometrics enrollment software has been im plemented in total 79 Indian 
Missions, Citizenship Amendment Act, 2015 was enacted on 11.03.2015 facilitating 
merger of PIO and OCI cards and easier acquisition of Indian Citizenship. 
(xxxviii) Various relaxations for long-term visa (LTV) holder s of Pakistani minorities have 
been approved. These include the facilitation for s tudies of their children, visit to 
additional places and employment in private sector,  easy renewal of LTVs and 
renewal for prolonged period.  
(xxxix) Digitisation of approximately 1.5 lakh files of his torical importance, pertaining to 
the freedom fighters has been done, out of which approximately 1,30,000 have been 
uploaded/hosted on the NIC server. 
(xl)  Electronic database of more than 1.2 billion perso ns created under National 
Population Register (NPR). 
(xli) Biometrics have been captured for 19.23 crore out o f total targeted population (5yr 
+) of 20.72 crore in ten out of twelve States/UTs a llocated to Office of Registrar 
General of India (ORGI). 
2.15 The Committee notes the overall security situa tion in the country particularly in the 
North-Eastern States and Left Wing Extremism (LWE) affected States and the efforts to 
bring ultras from North East to negotiating table.  The Committee is of the view that the 
Ministry should enhance its efforts to bring these separatist forces to the main stream 
through dialogue and negotiation.   
 
2.16 The Committee simultaneously expresses its ang uish on the grievous breach of 
security in Pathankot and Gurdaspur (Punjab) and fe els that these incidents put a question 
mark on our security preparedness and reflects of g aps in our intelligence network.  The 
Committee observes that these incidents put shadow on all other achievements made on the 
internal security front and hence impresses upon th e Ministry to take all steps to see that 
such incidents are not repeated in future.      
 
2.17 The Committee notes that the procedure for Sec urity Clearance of proposals relating 
to Defence/ Infrastructure Projects   and Maintenan ce Policy for the Border Infrastructure 

21 
 
(i.e. Border Roads, Flood Lights, Border Fencing an d Border Outposts) has been finalized.  
The Committee would like to be apprised of the same.   
 
2.18 The Committee also notes that the Government h as approved 33% reservation for 
women in direct recruitment for Non-Gazetted posts from Constable to Sub-Inspector in the 
CAPFs & police forces of all the UTs including Delh i Police.  The Committee hopes that 
assured 33% recruitment of women in these forces wi ll instill confidence among women folk 
and bridge gender divide prevalent in the society.  It should be implemented at the earliest. 
 
2.19 The Committee notes that the 78% (Approx 12000 ) Police stations are registering 
100% FIR and an advisory to ensure no discrimination in registering FIRs was issued on 12 
th  
August, 2015. However, the Committee doubts this cl aim and therefore, would like to know 
the basis used to arrive at this claim that 78% (Ap prox 12000) Police stations are registering 
100% FIR.  However, efforts should be continued to achieve this in all police stations.  
 
2.20 The Committee is happy to note that various re laxations for long-term visa (LTV) 
holders of Pakistani minorities have been approved which include the facilitation for studies 
of their children, visit to additional places and e mployment in private sector, easy renewal of 
LTVs and renewal for prolonged period. The Committe e is hopeful that this relaxation for 
Pakistani minorities residing in India will help in their rehabilitation.   
 
2.21 The Ministry was asked about priorities of the  Government in the current financial year 
in terms of improving Internal Security scenario in  the country. The Ministry in its written reply 
has given following replies:-  
 
(a) The principal focus on the Internal Security front remained on countering terrorism 
in Jammu& Kashmir, North Eastern Areas, Left Wing E xtremism affected States 
and the hinterland of the Country. 
 
(b)  Government of India in tandem with the State Gover nment of Jammu and Kashmir 
have adopted a multi-pronged approach to contain cross border infiltration, promote 
economic activity, provide relief to victims and st rengthen the State Government 
apparatus. 
 
(c) The Government is similarly following a multi-prong ed strategy to deal with 
insurgent and militant activities in the North East  Region. Priority includes a 
willingness to talk to groups, provided they abjure  violence, seek resolution of their 
demands within the frame work of the Indian Constit ution and come into the main 
stream of national life. Union Government is also s upplementing the efforts of the 
State Governments through various measures such as deployment of Central 
Security Forces to assist the State Authorities in counter insurgency operations and 
providing security to vulnerable institutions and i nstallations based on threat 
assessment.  
(d) The Government is dealing with Left Wing Extremism (LWE) in a holistic manner. 
Priority includes development, ensuring rights and entitlements of local 
communities and public perception management.  Cent ral Government is closely 

22 
 
monitoring the situation and assisting State Govern ments through providing 
CAPFs, Commando Battalions for Resolute Action (CoB RA), sanction of India 
Reserve Battalions, setting up of Counter Insurgenc y and Anti Terrorism (CIAT) 
School, modernization and upgradation of the State Police Forces, re-imbursement 
of security related expenditure  under the Security  Related Expenditure (SRE), 
providing helicopter for anti-LWE operations, shari ng intelligence, facilitation of 
inter-State coordination etc. 
(e) The Government remains committed to disrupt and dis mantle the funding of 
terrorism.  
2.22   Regarding Other Thrust areas for the current  financial year, the Ministry has informed as 
under:- 
(a)  Border Management- to curb infiltration, smuggling  and other cross border 
criminal activities. 
(b) Erection of fencing along with patrol roads, floodl ighting and construction of 
Border Out Posts. 
(c) Construction of roads, along the Indo Pak and Indo Bangladesh Border, Indo 
Nepal. 
(d) Construction of Integrated Check Posts (ICPs) on borders. 
(e) Strengthening of Costal Security viz. establishment  of vessel tracking and 
monitoring system, issuance of ID cards to fishermen etc. 
(f) To strengthen national capability to effectively manage disasters by developing 
a holistic, proactive, multi-disaster oriented and technology driven strategy 
through a culture of prevention, mitigation, preparedness and response. 
2.23          The Committee notes that the prime fo cus area of the Central Government on the 
Internal Security front will be countering terroris m in Jammu & Kashmir, North Eastern 
Areas, Left Wing Extremism affected States and the hinterland of the Country.  The 
Committee also notes that the Government of India i n tandem with the State Government of 
Jammu and Kashmir have adopted a multi-pronged appr oach to check cross border 
infiltration, promote economic growth, provide reli ef to victims and strengthen the State 
Government apparatus. 
2.24       The Committee also notes that the Govern ment proposes to adopt multi-pronged 
strategy to deal with extremists/separatists of Nor th East Region.  The Committee is of the 
view that the Government should start dialogue with  these groups and address 
sympathetically their grievances within the frame w ork of the Constitution of India with an 
aim to bring them into the main stream.  The multip ronged strategy should include this 
aspect also.  
2.25       The Committee also notes that the priori ty of Central Government in dealing with 
Left Wing Extremism (LWE) is development, ensuring rights and entitlements of local 
communities and public perception management. The C ommittee is of the view that 
development measures should have precedence over se curity measures while dealing with 
Left Wing Extremists through security measures cannot be ignored.

Notes: 1. Abbreiviation : RGI- Registrar General and Census Commission, India , NPR- National Population Register, 
J&K, Jammu and Kashmir, IVFRT- Mission Mode Project  (MMP) on Immigration, Visa and Foreigners Registra tion 
and Tracking, BM- Border Management, BADP- Border A rea Development Programme, CAPF-IMS- Central Armed 
Police Forces- Institute of Medical Sciences, NCRMP - National Cyclone Risk Mitigation Project, ODMP- O ther 
Disaster Management Project, NDMTI- National Disast er Management Training Institute, NDRF- National Di saster 
Response Force, LWE- Left Wing Extremism, NATGRID- Natgrid Intelligence Grid.2. Total Plan allocation for the 
12th Five Year Plan (2012-13 to 2016-17) is Rs. 600 69.39 cr. including Rs. 52839 cr. for all the Plan Schemes of 
MHA and Rs. 7230 cr. for BADP, About 94 Plan Scheme s are being implemented in MHA. Some of the scheme are 
still not approved. 3. Among the Plan schemes, MHA has been implementing eight Centrally Sponsored Sch emes 
(CSS) of MHA (namely Civil Defence, Fire & Emergenc y, NCRMP, ODMP, CCTNS, Special Infrastruture Scheme  
for LWE CIAT and MoPF), during the 12th Five Year P lan. During 2012-13 and 2013-14. During 2012-13 and  2013-
14, the CSS of Civil Defence and Fire and Emergency  were covered under the Grant No. 53 (MHA). The CSS  of 
NCRMP and ODMP were covered under Grant No.56 durin g 2012-13 & 2013-14. The other CSS were covered und er 
the Grant No.55 (Police) during these years. The Pl anning Commission (Now NITI AAYOG) made restructuri ng of 
the CSS of the GOI vide their communication dated 1 1.7.2013 wef 2014-15. Accordingly, all the eight CS S of MHA 
during the 12th Plan were clubbed together in one U mbrella scheme called National Scheme for Moderniza tion and 
Police and other Forces.  Based on the restructurin g of the CSS, this Umbrella Scheme in MHA covering all the above 
said eight CSS of MHA were covered in Grant No. 55 during 2014-15. However, the Annual Plan Budget was  made 
by the M/o Finance during 2015-16 instead of the NI TI AAYOG earlier. Accordingly, MOF did not give any  fund for 
the CSS of MHA included in the Umbrella Scheme exce pt the Externally Aided Project of NCRMP. According ly, the 
NCRMP has been shown in Grant No. 56 again wef 2015 -16. Certain amount has been shown  by MHA in BE ag ainst 
CCTNS and Civil Defence during 2015-16 for UTs. 4. BADP has been transferred by the Planning Commissio n from 
M/o Finance to MHA w.e.f 2014-15 under the restructuring of the CSS.  
2.27 The Committee is displeased to note the under expenditure in respect of many of the 
plan schemes of the Ministry of Home Affairs consis tently during the years 2012-16 (as on 
29 th  February, 2016).   The main concern of the Committ ee is that during the last four 
financial years, the Ministry has not even been abl e to spend the reduced allocation at RE 
Stage in several schemes which is an indicator of u nder-performances.  The Committee notes 
that under many schemes/projects like infrastructur e for CAPFs or for Border Management 
where there are still huge gaps in what is desired and what is existing.  Moreover, the funds 
available have not been optimally utilized.  The Mi nistry needs to gear up its machinery to 
analyze what is the cause of this consistent under- utilisation and take adequate measure to 
plug the gaps for achieving the desired objectives and goals.  The Committee would like to be 
apprised about the steps to strengthen the absorpti on capacity of various schemes and 
agencies. Besides, the Committee has apprehension t hat this underutilization may prove to 
be a valid ground for further reduction in allocation during future financial allocations.   The 
Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry of Home Affairs should pay attention 
to restructuring its implementation agencies so that the schemes /projects could be completed 
without time and cost overrun.

CHAPTER – III 
DEMAND NO. 46 & 47  
3.1 Demand No. 46 – Ministry of Home Affairs  
 
3.1.1 The Demand No. 46 pertains to expenditure to be incurred on Ministry of Home Affairs 
Secretariat, Office of the Registrar General of Ind ia (RGI) for Census activities, various offices 
functioning under the Department of Official Langua ge, Civil Defence, Home Guards, National 
Human Rights Commission, Fire Services, Departmenta l Accounting Organization of MHA and 
National Cyclone Risk Mitigation Project with World  Bank assistance.   Under this Demand, the 
provision on account of rehabilitation of repatriat es/migrants from other countries and from the 
State of J&K, Pension and other benefits of Freedom  Fighters, Helicopter subsidies for North East 
and Various other schemes of Disaster Management ha ve been incorporated from this Financial 
Year 2016-17.  Besides, from this Financial Year, t he earlier Grant No. 56 (Others Expenditure of 
MHA) and Grant No. 53 (Ministry of Home Affairs) ha ve been merged and has now been named 
as Grant No. 46- Ministry of Home Affairs.  The bud getary provision of the Demand for 2016-17 
is given in the following table:-

3.1.2 The Ministry has informed that the decrease o f Rs. 237.20 crore in RE 2015-16 over BE 
2015-16 under 'Non-Plan' was mainly due to mandator y cut imposed by Finance Ministry, non 
filling up of the posts, less receipts of bills fro m Overseas Citizens of India (OCI) Cell, Foreigners' 
Division, MHA, non finalization of proposal under v arious Schemes of National Disaster 
Response Force, non finalization of proposal of shi fting of campus of National Fire Service 
college, Nagpur, Registration and surveillance of F oreigners, non finalization of rehabilitation 
package of displaced Kashmiri Migrants which includ e providing 3000 Government Jobs to 
Kashmiri Migrants and construction of transit accommodation in the Valley.   
 
3.1.3 The Committee was informed that the increase of Rs. 514.61 crore in BE 2016-17 in 
comparison to  RE 2015-16 has been made on account of various reasons such as hike in Salaries, 
DA and Arrears and to accommodate anticipated incre ase due to implementation of 
recommendation of 7
th  Central Pay Commission.  According to the Ministry , the increase has also 
been affected under the Infrastructure Schemes of N ational Disaster Response Forces and also to 
meet the expenses on engagement of casual workers a nd purchase of stationery etc., Rent, Rates 
and Taxes making the payment of rent to NDCC-II bui lding, due increase in the provisions under 
Salaries, Office Expenses due to increase in the ra te of electricity Bills/Water Bills etc. and 

32 
 
Professional services claims and other establishmen t related expenditure of NDRF and for 
Capacity Building Programmes of Disaster Management, funds required pertaining to new campus 
of NFSC, Nagpur including furnishing and procuremen t of furniture and lab equipments and also 
procurement of motor vehicles as training aids. 
 
3.1.4 The Ministry has submitted that the increase of Rs. 58.26 crore in RE 2015-16 over BE 
2015-16 under 'Plan' was mainly due to increase in the provisions under Salaries, Officer Expenses 
due to increase in the rate of electricity Bills/Wa ter Bills etc. and Professional services claims of 
Allowance to Enumerators and to make payment to outside agencies for CAC work, also enhanced 
provision for Police Medal and Advertisement & Publ icity Campaign in  J&K, provision is 
increased for purchases pertaining to new campus of  National Fire Service College (NFSC), 
Nagpur including furnishing and procurement of furn iture and lab equipments and also 
procurement of motor vehicles as training aids. 
 
3.1.5 The Ministry has further submitted that there  is an increase of Rs. 159.74 crores in BE 
2016-17 with reference to RE 2015-16. During 2016-1 7, the increase is mainly due to merger of 
Other Expenditure of MHA Grant with Grant No. 46 of  MHA and increase in Salaries and other 
Establishment related expenses for all organization  under the grant, probable increase under salary 
head and implementation of various schemes under Of ficial Language, actual requirement under 
National Population Register, increase in the provi sions under Salaries and other administrative 
expenses, enhanced provision of Advertisement & Pub licity Campaign in North East and  LWE 
affected areas, start of Phase-II programme of NCRMP and expenditure for NDRF infrastructure. 
  
3.1.6 The Committee takes note of the various reaso n cited for decrease of Rs. 237.20 crore 
in RE 2015-16 over BE 2015-16 under 'Non-Plan' and expresses its displeasure that non 
finalization of proposal under various Schemes of N ational Disaster Response Force, non 
finalization of proposal of shifting of campus of N ational Fire Service college, Nagpur and 
non finalization of rehabilitation package of displ aced Kashmiri Migrants which included 
providing 3000 Government Jobs to Kashmiri Migrants  and construction of transit 
accommodation in the Valley are some of the reasons  for this decrease.  In view of the above, 
the Committee recommends that the Ministry should d well into the reasons for these delays 
in many important schemes/projects under the Head a nd put its machinery into gear to fast 
pace finalization of these schemes/packages at the earliest.

3.1.8 The Committee takes a serious view of the und er expenditure under this grant during 
the year 2015-16.  The Committee is constrained to note that the Ministry was allocated Rs. 
1570 crore under Demand No. 53 (which is now known as Demand No. 46) which was 
increased to 1751.16 crore at RE stage in the year 2015-16 but the Ministry has been able to 
spend only Rs. 1303.17 crore at the fag end of the last fiscal.  It reflects low absorption 
capacity of the Ministry under the scheme and will weaken its case before the Finance 
Ministry while arguing for augmentation in allocation.

3.1.10 The Committee observes that against projecte d demand of Rs. 5003.97 crore under 
Demand No. 46 only Rs. 4630.90 crore (Rs. 1250.00 c rore under Plan and Rs. 3380.90 crore 

34 
 
Non-Plan) has been allocated for the year 2016-17.  The Committee also observes that there 
is a shortfall in major Heads such as Secretariat-G eneral Services, Official language, 
Disaster Management, Civil Defence and Fire and Eme rgency Service, Home Guards, etc. 
The Committee presumes that this shortfall in alloc ation is a clear indicator of low 
absorption of funds as highlighted in the trend of expenditure during last financial year and 
hence impresses upon the Ministry to optimize its u tilization capacity to put up a robust case 
for more allocations at RE stage.  
 
 
Civil Defence 
 
 
3.2.1 The Ministry was asked about measures adopted  for revitalization of Civil Defence during 
2015-16.  The Ministry in its reply has informed th at it is the primary responsibility of the State 
Governments to allocate sufficient resources for st rengthening and equipping Civil Defence set up 
in the area of their jurisdiction.  The Ministry ha s further informed that in 2015-16, there was no 
central financial assistance to the States for revi talization of Civil Defence since all the Centrally  
Sponsored schemes have been subsumed in the State P lan Funds w.e.f. 1 
st  April, 2015. This is on 
account of higher share of allocation to States in the 14 th  Finance Commission, and the states have 
additional resources to carry out the plan schemes and they can implement the schemes as per their 
own requirement.  
 
3.2.2 The Ministry has added that till 2014-15 the Government of India extended financial 
support under the schemes for Revamping of Civil De fence in the country (2009-2013) at an 
outlay of Rs 100 crores and Mainstreaming Civil Def ence in Disaster Risk Reduction at a total 
outlay of Rs. 290.89 crore for implementation durin g 12 th  Plan period.  The Ministry has further 
added that an amount of Rs. 15 crore was released t o States during 2014-15 and since the scheme 
has been subsumed in the State Sector, no budgetary provision for the States has been made during 
2015-16.  
3.2.3 The Ministry was asked about measures needed for revitalization of Civil Defence.  The 
Ministry has suggested following measures for Revitalization of Civil Defence Setup: 
(i) As on date, only 40 percent of total strength of CD  volunteers exists in the country. 
Therefore, more awareness programs, motivational ac tivities and enrolment related 
activities should be planned to augment the numbers of CD volunteers in the states. 
 
(ii) The activities related to training of CD Volunteers , creation of infrastructure for CD 
organizations and involvement of CD volunteers into  Disaster Management activities 
should be implemented for overall strengthening of these organizations. 
 
3.2.4 The Committee takes serious view of the fact that during 2015-16, there was no 
central financial assistance to the States for revi talization of Civil Defence since all the 
Centrally Sponsored schemes have been subsumed in t he State Plan Funds since 1 
st  April, 
2015. The Committee is not satisfied with the argum ent put forward by the Ministry to 
justify scrapping of central support on the ground of so called higher share of allocation to 
States in the 14 th  Finance Commission.  The Committee is unable to un derstand why the 
Central Government in a federal set up is abdicatin g its responsibilities and leaving the State 

35 
 
Governments in a lurch.  The Committee is aware of the fact that some State Governments 
are already hard pressed even in terms of paying sa laries to their employees, etc and it is 
almost impossible for them to manage additional res ources to carry out the plan schemes as 
per their own requirement.  Therefore, the Committe e recommends that the Government 
must continue to give financial assistance to the States for revitalisation of Civil Defence.   
 
Fire & Emergency Services 
 
3.3.1 The Ministry was asked to furnish detailed no te on fire and emergency service in the 
country.  The Ministry in its written reply has sub mitted that 'Fire Services’ is a State subject and 
has been included as municipal function in the XII th schedule (Serial Number 7 of Article 243 (W)) 
of the Constitution of India and it is the primary responsibility of the State Governments to ensure 
safety of life and property of the citizens in the area of their jurisdiction by providing adequate 
number of fire stations, fire tenders and trained m anpower.  According to the Ministry, the 
structure of fire services is not uniform in the Co untry. Fire Services in the States of Gujarat, 
Chhattisgarh, Punjab, Maharashtra, Himachal Pradesh , Haryana and Madhya Pradesh excluding 
Indore are under the respective Municipal Corporati ons. In other remaining States it is under the 
Home Department.  
3.3.2 The Ministry has further submitted that Fire Services in most of the States fall within the 
jurisdiction of local bodies, which are often starv ed of financial resources and as a result the Fire 
Services have generally remained ill equipped.  
3.3.3 Regarding role of Central Government, the Min istry has added that the Centre  has 
supplemented the efforts of the State Governments b y way of providing Financial and Technical 
support under the Centrally Sponsored Schemes, as mentioned below:- 
(i)  Scheme for ‘Strengthening of Fire & Emergency Services in the Country’ (2009-13) 
with a total outlay of Rs. 200 crore. Rs. 176.56 crore were released to the States during 
the period of 2009-2013.  
(ii) Scheme for ‘Modernization of Fire and Emergenc y Services’ (2014-15) with a total 
outlay of Rs. 75 crore. Rs. 30 crore were released during the period of 2014-15 to the 
States.  
3.3.4 According to the Ministry, since the Governme nt of India has accepted the 
recommendations of 14 
th  Finance Commission to devolve 42 percent of the di visible pool to the 
States during award period 2015-2016  to 2019-2020 in place of 32 percent, as such all the 
Centrally Sponsored Schemes have now been subsumed in State Funds and accordingly, State 
Governments are required to suitably address/undert ake all necessary measures for strengthening 
of Fire Services related activities and allocate su fficient funds for carrying out such activities as 
per their specific requirements.   
3.3.5 The Committee takes note of the submission of  the Ministry that Fire Services in most 
of the States fall within the jurisdiction of local  bodies which are often starved of financial 
resources hence ill-equipped.  The Committee also t akes note of the submission of the 
Ministry that financial constraints have also been a major factor for the State Governments 

36 
 
for neglecting Fire Services. The Committee is at p ains to note that the Central Government 
has discontinued Scheme for ‘Modernization of Fire and Emergency Services’ in the name of 
14 th  Finance Commission recommendations which according  to Committee are not 
practicable. The Committee in view of this recommen ds that a serious introspection is 
needed by the Central Government in this regard to avoid the problems which might emerge 
due to discontinuation of many centrally sponsored schemes.    
Relief & Rehabilitation of Migrants and repatriates 
3.4.1 The Ministry was asked about amount utilized till 29 th  February, 2016 out of the allocation 
made in RE 2015-16 under Relief & Rehabilitation of Migrants and repatriates (Major Head 3601).  
The Ministry in its reply has submitted that out of  allocation of Rs.188.03 crore in RE 2015-16, an 
amount of Rs. 96.24 crore has been utilized till 29 th  February, 2016 and another 90 crore is under 
process for the scheme of rehabilitation package fo r the returnees from former Indian enclaves in 
Bangladesh and upgradation of infrastructure in for mer Bangladeshi enclaves in India.  The 
Committee was informed that another installment of Rs.1.76 crore was also under process for 
reimbursement to the Government of Tamil Nadu for p roviding relief assistance to the Sri Lankan 
Tamil refugees staying in camps. 
3.4.2 The Ministry has further submitted that the a ctual expenditure till February 2016 is 
Rs.174.22 crore out of the allocation of Rs.280 cro re made in RE 2015-16for relief and 
rehabilitation of Kashmiri migrants under Security Related Expenditure (Relief and 
Rehabilitation)-SRE(R&R). 
3.4.3 Besides, Rs. 20 Crore and Rs. 15 Crore were a llocated for grants-in-aid to the Government 
of Tripura and Mizoram respectively for maintenance  of Bru migrants in Tripura and rehabilitation 
& resettlement of Brus in Mizoram in RE 2015-16.  A ccording to the Ministry, out of above 
allocated amount, Rs. 19.19 Crore and 5 Crore have been released to the Governments of Tripura 
and Mizoram, respectively, by 29 
th  February, 2016 and remaining fund available under the scheme 
is expected to be utilized by end of current financial year. 
3.4.4 The Ministry has also informed that the total  expenditure incurred towards payment of 
additional compensation to the next of kin of those  killed in 1984 riots as on  29 th  February, 2016 
is Rs. 3.90 crore. 
3.4.5 The Committee is dismayed to note that the Mi nistry has been able to spend 
approximately 50% (till 29 
th  February, 2016) of allocation made in RE 2015-16 u nder Relief 
& Rehabilitation of Migrants and repatriates(Major Head 3601) as it has utilized only Rs. 
96.24 crore out of allocation of Rs.188.03 crore in  RE 2015-16.  The Committee is not 
satisfied with the submission that Rs. 90 crore is under process for the scheme of 
rehabilitation package for the returnees from forme r Indian enclaves in Bangladesh and 
upgradation of infrastructure in former Bangladeshi  enclaves in India and observes that 
months have passed since enclaves were exchanged wi th Bangladesh but rehabilitation 
package for the Indian enclave dwellers is still no t processed.  Besides, the Committee hopes 
that installment of Rs. 1.76 crore for reimbursemen t to the Government of Tamil Nadu for 
providing relief assistance to the Sri Lankan Tamil refugees will be released soon.  

37 
 
3.4.6 The Ministry was asked to give details of sch emes/projects proposed to be undertaken for 
rehabilitation of J&K migrants in coming years.   T he Ministry in its reply has informed that the 
Government has approved a package, on 18 th  November 2015, involving an estimated expenditure 
of Rs.2000 Crore for providing financial assistance  for additional 3000 state government jobs to 
the Kashmiri migrants and construction of 6000 transit accommodations in the Kashmir Valley and  
the State Government of J&K has been requested to t ake necessary action to implement this 
proposal at the earliest.  According to the Ministr y, it has been informed by the State Government 
that 3000 posts have been identified in 16 differen t Departments and necessary action is being 
taken for creation of these posts.  Besides, author ization has been issued to executing agency 
(JKPCC) for construction of 608 transit accommodations and land is being identified for remaining 
transit accommodations.  
3.4.7 The Ministry has submitted that the Governmen t has also approved a proposal, involving a 
total estimated expenditure of Rs.13.45 crore per a nnum, to provide relief to the migrants of the 
hilly areas of Jammu division [who migrated from di fferent hilly areas of Jammu division to safer 
places of nearby towns due to security reasons] at par with the Kashmiri migrants i.e. cash relief of 
Rs.2500 per head per month (maximum Rs.10000 per fa mily per month) plus relief for 
ration/fodder.  According to the Ministry, at prese nt there are about 1054 registered migrant 
families who are living in various parts of the Jam mu region of the State.   The Ministry has also 
submitted that the State Government was providing c ash relief of Rs.400 per head per month plus 
ration to such families from its own resources, how ever, the Government of India would now be 
funding the relief (cash and ration/fodder) for migrants of Jammu region fulfilling the long pending 
demand of the migrants by ensuring uniformity in th e relief package for both the regions.  The 
Ministry has added that the State Government has is sued necessary order for implementing the 
proposal vide no. R&R/MR/266/2015 dated 19-01-2016. 
3.4.8 Regarding measures taken over the years by th e Government for rehabilitation of Kashmiri 
migrants, the Ministry has apprised the Committee t hat  under Prime Minister’s Package of the 
year 2004, 5242 two room tenements at four location s in Jammu (Purkhoo, Muthi, Nagrota and 
Jagti)  and 200 flats at Sheikhpora in Budgam distr ict (Kashmir Valley) have been constructed and 
allotted to the migrants.   Besides, a Comprehensiv e Package was announced by the Government 
in 2008 for return and rehabilitation of Kashmiri M igrants, which provided for 3000 state 
government jobs, financial assistance for purchase/  construction of houses, construction of transit 
accommodations, continuation of cash relief to migr ants, scholarship to students, assistance for 
self-employment, assistance to the agriculturists a nd the horticulturists, waiver of interest on 
unpaid loan, etc. According to the Ministry, the Pa ckage is being implemented by the State 
Government of Jammu and Kashmir. So far state gover nment jobs have been offered to 1963 
migrant youths, out of which 1719 have joined and 5 05 Transit accommodations have been 
constructed in the Kashmir Valley and allotted to migrant employees under the Package. 
3.4.9 The Committee takes serious view of the fact that Rs.174.22 crore only was spent till 
February 2016 out of the allocation of Rs.280 crore  made in RE 2015-16 for relief and 
rehabilitation of Kashmiri migrants under Security Related Expenditure.  The Committee 
would like the Ministry to find out the reasons for  under-utilisation of funds for a scheme 
which calls for immediate attention and take all me asures to hasten the pace of expenditure.  
The Committee would like to be apprised of the detailed action plan drawn in this regard.  

38 
 
3.4.10 However, the Committee simultaneously notes that the Government has approved a 
package in November 2015, involving an estimated ex penditure of Rs.2000 Crore for 
providing financial assistance for additional 3000 state government jobs to the Kashmiri 
migrants and construction of 6000 transit accommoda tions in the Kashmir Valley. The 
Committee hopes that the Ministry would take up wit h the J&K State Government to fast 
pace implementation of this package in terms of pro viding jobs and construction of transit 
accommodation for Kashmiri Migrants.   
3.4.11 The Committee also notes that the Government  has approved a proposal with 
estimated expenditure of Rs.13.45 crore per annum, to provide relief to the migrants of the 
hilly areas of Jammu division (who migrated from di fferent hilly areas of Jammu division to 
safer places of nearby towns due to security reasons) at par with the Kashmiri migrants.  The 
Committee appreciates that they will be provided wi th cash relief of Rs.2500 per head per 
month (maximum Rs.10000 per family per month) plus relief for ration/fodder.  The 
Committee is hopeful that this relief measure is im plemented in a time bound manner.  The 
Committee would like to be informed about the steps  proposed to be taken to fast track these 
rehabilitation moves of the Government.  
 
Disaster Management 
 
 
3.5.1 Under the Head 'Disaster Management' Rs. 919. 29 crore was allocated in BE 2015-16 
which was reduced to Rs. 650.99 crore at RE stage i n that financial year.  The Ministry was asked 
about the reasons behind this decrease.   The Minis try in its written reply has informed that the 
reduction in RE 2015-16 is mainly attributable to t he delay in undertaking the works of 
development of infrastructure of various NDRF batta lions.  According to the Ministry, after EFC 
appraisal in March 2015, initiative was taken to aw ard works to the PWOs for carrying out the 
developmental activities. Due to delay in preparati on of Detailed Project Reports, the proposals 
were received in the Ministry for administrative ap proval/expenditure sanction at a very late stage. 
However, as of now, works of Office Buildings and R esidential Quarters have been awarded to 
PWOs. The utilization of funds against the administ rative sanction orders is anticipated in first 
quarter of Financial Year 2016-17. 
3.5.2 The National Cyclone Risk Mitigation Project (NCRMP) is a World Bank assisted 
Centrally Sponsored Scheme with a view to address t he vulnerability of coastal community to 
cyclones who are generally poor and are from weaker  section of the society.  This project aims to 
minimize the vulnerability to cyclone and making pe ople and infrastructure disaster resilient.  The 
Ministry was asked about status of implementation o f National Cyclone Risk Mitigation Project.  
The Ministry in its reply has apprised the Committe e that the project has four principal 
components namely: 
(i) Component A – Last Mile Connectivity 
(ii) Component B – Structural and Non-Structural Measures 
(iii) Component C - Technical Assistance for Cyclone Hazard Risk Mitigation,   
           Capacity Building and Knowledge Creation  
(iv) Component D – Project Management and Implementation Support. 
 

39 
 
3.5.3 According to the Ministry, Components A, C & D will be fully financed by the Central 
Government through World Bank assistance and compon ent B will be financed by Central and 
State Governments in the ratio of 75:25.   The Mini stry has also informed that the objective of the 
Project is to strengthen the structural and non str uctural mitigation infrastructure. The Project is 
being implemented with effect from January, 2011 in  the States of Andhra Pradesh and Odisha by 
NDMA at a total outlay of Rs. 1496.71 Cr. with Worl d Bank assistance to the tune of Rs. 1198.44 
Cr. (Central share) and States share to the tune of Rs. 298.27 Cr.  
 
3.5.4 The Ministry has further informed that the co st of the Project has since been revised to Rs. 
2331.71 Cr. during July, 2015 by the Government of India to reduce the infrastructural gaps 
observed during the handling of cyclones Phailin (2 013) and Hudhud (2014) by creating 
Additional Infrastructure. Now the Government of In dia will provide assistance of Rs. 1843.94 
crore through a World Bank loan and the remaining amount of Rs. 487.77 crore will be contributed 
by the State Governments of Andhra Pradesh and Odis ha. The scheme which was approved up to 
31st January, 2016 has now been extended to 31st March, 2018 with same components.  
3.5.5 According to the Ministry, till date, 232 Cyc lone Shelters, 723.48 km of roads, 19 bridges 
and 2 saline embankments have been completed under NCRMP Phase-I.  
3.5.6 Regarding NCRMP Phase-II, the Ministry has fu rnished following information:- 
(i) The Government of India has also approved Phase-II of NCRMP in July, 2015 
for implementation in the States of Goa, Gujarat, K arnataka, Kerala, 
Maharashtra and West Bengal at an outlay of Rs. 236 1.35 Cr. with the World 
Bank assistance amounting to Rs. 1881.20 Cr. The re maining amount of Rs. 
480.15 Cr. is to be contributed by State Government s as their share on the 
pattern of NCRMP Phase-I. The Project is to be impl emented by NDMA for a 
period of 5 years upto 31.03.2020.  
(ii) While the States of Gujarat and West Bengal have made physical progress in the 
matter regarding award of work for construction of shelters, the remaining 
States of Goa, Karnataka, Kerala and Maharashtra ar e in the process of 
finalization of Sites, Environment clearances, DPR, etc.  
(iii) A total of approximately Rs. 141 Cr. has been relea sed to States of Gujarat, 
Karnataka, Kerala, Maharashtra and West Bengal as f irst installment under 
NCRMP-II. 
(iv) The construction of 180 Cyclone Shelters and 70 Kms . roads has been awarded 
by the State of Gujarat and West Bengal under NCRMP Phase-II. 
 
3.5.7 The Ministry was also asked about measures ta ken and proposed to be taken to rehabilitate 
people affected by Chennai flood.  The Ministry in its reply submitted that the primary 
responsibility for disaster management rests with t he States and the concerned State Governments 
undertake relief & rehabilitation measures in the w ake of natural disasters from the State Disaster 
Response Fund (SDRF) already placed at their dispos al in accordance with the Government of 
India approved items and norms of assistance.  According to the Ministry, the Government of India 
supplements their efforts by providing assistance f rom the National Disaster Response Fund 

40 
 
(NDRF) by following the laid down procedure. Financ ial assistance under SDRF/NDRF in the 
wake of natural disasters is by way of relief and not for compensation of loss as suffered/claimed. 
3.5.8 The Ministry has further submitted that regar ding rehabilitation of people, who are affected 
by the natural calamities including flood, action i s undertaken by the concerned State Government 
from its own resources/Plan funds, and also based o n the availability of Government land as per 
their existing land policy and the State Government  is providing relief assistance and has taken 
initiatives to address the issue of rehabilitation as per its policy. However, the status of the 
rehabilitation measures taken by the State Government is not maintained centrally. 
3.5.9 The Ministry has also informed that in order to support the relief measures undertaken for 
the people of Tamil Nadu, affected in the recent fl ood in 2015, the Government of India had 
released an amount of Rs. 1000 crore, ‘on account’ basis from NDRF to the State Government and 
an overall amount of Rs. 2195.45 crore has been rel eased to the State to deal with the calamity so 
far.  
3.5.10 The Committee is constrained to note that Rs . 919.29 crore allocated under the Head 
'Disaster Management' in BE 2015-16 was reduced to Rs. 650.99 crore at RE stage due to 
delay in undertaking the works of development of infrastructure of various NDRF battalions.  
The Committee does not want to go into procedural wrangles leading to delay in taking off of 
any project and recommends that procedural complexi ties should be minimized to fast pace 
the project concerning development of infrastructur e of various NDRF battalions.  The 
Committee would like to be apprised of the utilisat ion status of funds in the first quarter of 
the current financial year against the administration sanction order.      
3.5.11 The Committee takes note of the mandate of W orld Bank assisted Centrally 
Sponsored Scheme National Cyclone Risk Mitigation P roject (NCRMP) whose objective is to 
address the vulnerability of coastal community to c yclones who are generally poor and are 
from weaker section of the society through minimizi ng the vulnerability to cyclone and 
making people and infrastructure disaster resilient .  The Committee hopes that this project 
will be completed in a time bound manner and go a l ong way in terms of establishing robust 
mitigation infrastructure to with stand cyclone.  T he Committee may be periodically 
apprised of the status of different projects under this scheme and also of the steps being 
taken to avoid any element of delay.  
3.5.12 The Committee is aware of the fact that the concerned affected State Governments 
have primary responsibility for disaster management  in the event of occurrence of any 
disaster and the States with their limited resource s undertake relief & rehabilitation 
measures in the wake of natural disasters.  The Com mittee recommends that the Central 
Government should play a major role in the event of  big disasters taking place in any State.   
The Committee also recommends that the Central Gove rnment should set up a monitoring 
mechanism to oversee the status of the rehabilitati on measures taken by the State 
Government without undue interference.

POLICE FORCES 
Demand No. 48 – Police 
4.1.1 Demand No. 48 is considered to be the most im portant Demand pertaining to the Ministry 
of Home Affairs.   This Demand generally has the hi ghest allocation and meets expenditure on the 
Central Armed Police Forces and other Central Polic e Organisations, Departmental Accounting 
Organisation of Central Armed Police Forces, Nation al Intelligence Grid, Delhi Police, 
Intelligence Bureau, Bureau of Immigration, Narcoti cs Control Bureau, National Investigation 
Agency, Land Ports Authority of India, as well as f or certain schemes administered by Ministry of 
Home Affairs, such as Modernisation of Police Force s, Border Management, Coastal Security, 
Security Related Expenditure, Special Infrastructure in Left Wing Extremist affected States, Crime 
& Criminal Tracking Network System (CCTNS), Mission  Mode Project on Immigration, Visa and 
Foreigners Registration & Tracking (IVFRT), Reimbur sement to States for deployment of 
battalions.  According to the Ministry, provision u nder Grant No. 48 is also used for Welfare 
Grants of CPMFs and Research, assistance to Naxal a ffected States, National Intelligence Grid 
(NATGRID), etc.   
 
4.1.2 The Ministry has informed that from the finan cial year 2011-12 onwards, provisions for 
Intelligence Bureau, Bureau of Immigration, Narcoti cs Control Bureau and National Investigation 
Agency have been made under this Demand. Besides, f rom the financial year 2013-14 Special 
Protection Group (SPG) have also been transferred t o this Grant. This change with the approval of 
Ministry of Finance has been given effect for the better budgeting of these organizations.
