NATGRID  
4.2.1 It was 26/11 terror attacks on Mumbai which l ed to the exposure of several weaknesses in 
India’s intelligence gathering and action networks.   It was then came the idea  of National 
Intelligence Grid (NATGRID) which is proposed to in tegrate 21 categories of data from agencies 
like Banks, Railways and airlines, Income tax depar tment, Credit card companies Visa and 
immigration etc. This combined data will be made av ailable to 11 central agencies including the 
R&AW, NIA, CBI, Directorate of Revenue Intelligence , Intelligence Bureau, Narcotics Control 
Bureau and Enforcement Directorate to help them prevent terrorist attacks and criminal activities. 
4.2.2 The proposed National Intelligence Grid  (NAT GRID) has been set up as an attached office 
of the Ministry of Home Affairs with the mandate to  implement a framework which will leverage 
Information Technology to connect approved User Age ncies (security/law enforcement) with 
designated Data Providers (Airlines, Banks, SEBI, R ailway, Telecom, etc) with a view to enhance 
the country’s counter-terrorism capability. NATGRID will automate the existing manual processes 
for collation of intelligence information by connec ting over 80 Data Providers and develop tools 
for making actionable intelligence available to Use r Agencies. Thus, NATGRID shall be able to 
provide information online for security agencies to  save time and shall also provide actionable 
intelligence in the form of alerts & tags as defined by security agencies.  
4.2.3 The Ministry was asked about uneven trend of allocation of funds meant for NATGRID as 
in BE 2015-16 under plan allocation Rs. 52.94 crore  was allocated for NATGRID which was 
reduced to 16.81 crore at RE 2015-16 stage and agai n the Plan allocation for this project in BE 
2016-17 stands at Rs 45.00 crore.  The Ministry in its written reply has informed that in January 
2015, MHA approved a New Implementation Plan (NIP) where it was decided to implement the 
project by engaging a group of consultants and subj ect matter experts through NICSI/NISG which 
would be a more cost effective option and more cond ucive as to maintaining strategic control.  
Further, In view of rapid advancements in technology over the last few years, a need was felt to re-
evaluate the architecture and implementation method ology to ensure that a State-of-the-Art and 
optimal solution is delivered as part of NATGRID.   According to the Ministry, in light of this, an 
Advisory Group was constituted by MHA in September 2015 comprising domain experts across 
various fields to review the Detailed Project Repor t, which had been prepared in 2010.  Further, 
four sub-Committees from the members of the Advisor y Group and co-opting some additional 
members from NIC, were constituted to evaluate and suggest solutions for specific problems 
relating to technology, manpower, and implementatio n, which may confront the project during the 
course of its implementation. The meetings of sub-C ommittees have since been completed and the 
recommendations are being placed before the Advisor y Group. Due to this reason, the project 
implementation has been delayed, resulting in reduction in RE 2015-16 from BE 2015-16.   
4.2.4 The Committee is aware that NATGRID was conce ived after the 2008 Mumbai 
Terror Attack which many in security establishments  believe, could have been averted but 
for delayed scattered intelligence.  It aims to bri ng to one platform intelligence from over 
20 agencies like railways, banks, IB, RAW, CBI, ED,  Central Board of Direct Taxes, etc.  
The NATRGID is expected to use cutting edge technol ogy to place all the details regarding 
telephone, residential, travel and banking of a sus pect on a common platform. However, 
the Committee is extremely disappointed at the slow progress.  NATGRID was conceived in 
2010-11 to be put in place in four phases.  Two pha ses were planned to be completed by 

49 
 
2014 at an estimated cost of Rs. 1100 crore.  It is disheartening to know that even phase one 
has not been completed till date.  
 
4.2.5 The Committee is also aware that construction  of a building to house NATGRID 
was given to NBCC after foundation stone was laid i n 2014 in South Delhi.  It has taken 
around 2 years to get clearance from the State Leve l Environment Impact Assessment 
Authority (SLEIAA), which was granted in November, 2015.  Though as per the Ministry's 
information, construction has begun w.e.f February,  2016,   the estimated cost of the 
proposed complex is Rs. 150 crore but the total bud get allocation in 2016-17 is only Rs. 45 
crore.  It is very clear to the Committee that Gove rnment has gone slow on the NATGRID 
project whereas it strongly feels that his project should be given priority in order to tackle 
high-tech terrorism which has become order of the d ay. Therefore, the Committee 
recommends that the Ministry must prepare a plan wi th clear timeframe within which all 
the four phases should be completed as per set targ et and if required, the Ministry should 
ask for enhanced allocation at RE stage.   
 
4.2.6 The Ministry was asked to inform the Committe e about projected demand of the Ministry 
for NATGRID.  The Ministry in its response apprised  the Committee that MHA had projected for 
year 2016-17 Rs.104.00 crore, out of which Rs.45.00  Cr is under the Revenue Section and the 
Capital Head ‘M&E’, and Rs.59.00 crores was under t he Capital Head “Major Works” grouped 
separately under the Head ‘13.Police Infrastructure  13.01 CAPFs and Central Police 
Organizations' in the Expenditure Budget 2016-17 (V olume 2- Notes on Demands for Grants). 
According to the Ministry, the demand for “Major Wo rks” is a conservative demand, keeping in 
view the earlier trend of savings in BE.  The Minis try has further apprised that the construction 
work at New Delhi will pick up and any additional f unds required in 2016-17 would be sought 
through Supplementary Demands for Grants.  
4.2.7 The Ministry has given details of the progres s on this project .  According to the Ministry, 
NATGRID Scheme has two sub-projects. One sub projec t aims at setting up of the NATGRID IT 
framework/solution, and the other one for establishment of the physical infrastructure on which the 
NATGRID IT framework will be hosted – consisting of Data Centre, Business Continuity Plan and 
Office Complex at New Delhi, Disaster Recovery Centre at Bengaluru.  
4.2.8 The Ministry has submitted that CCS in June, 2012 approved implementation of Horizon I 
and some elements of Horizon II of the IT Framework  sub-project envisaged in the Detailed 
Project Report (DPR) for an estimated cost of Rs. 1 002.97 Cr, which were to be implemented in a 
period of 24 months from the CCS approval.  Accordi ng the Ministry Project implementation 
period has been further extended up to 30th June, 2 017.   The status of sub-projects and measures 
taken for their speedy implementation are as below: 
 
a) The deployment of complete solution can commence on ly after the physical 
construction of Data Centre(s) at New Delhi and Ben galuru. As per the high 
security/privacy norms mandated by the CCS – NATGRI D’s Data Centre, Disaster 
Recovery Centre and Business Continuity Plan site a re central to NATGRID’s 
capability to securely store critical data and serv ice the User Agencies through high 
security channels. NATGRID was instructed that thes e facilities will have to be 

50 
 
constructed by National Building Corporation Compan y (NBCC) on a turnkey basis. 
While the civil construction at Bangalore site is g oing as per schedule and is likely to 
be completed by June, 2016, the construction at Del hi site could not start as South 
Delhi Municipal Corporation (SDMC) disputed Delhi D evelopment Authority 
(DDA)ownership of the land. The issue between DDA a nd SDMC has been resolved. 
After the transfer in July 2015 to SDMC the land co st of Rs.19.29 Cr paid to DDA in 
earlier years and further payment of the additional  land cost to SDMC of Rs.11.01 Cr 
on 28.12.2015, the allotted land has been handed ov er to NATGRID on 31.12.2015. 
The construction has started in February 2016. 
 
b) In view of rapid advancements in technology over th e last few years, a need was felt to 
re-evaluate the architecture and implementation met hodology for the IT Framework 
sub-project as proposed in the NATGRID DPR,which wa s prepared in 2010, to ensure 
that a State-of-the-Art and optimal solution is del ivered as part of NATGRID. In light 
of this, an Advisory Group was constituted by MHA i n September 2015 comprising 
domain experts across various fields to review the DPR. Further, four sub-Committees 
from the members of the Advisory Group and co-opting some additional members from 
NIC, were constituted to evaluate and suggest solut ions for specific problems relating 
to technology, manpower, and implementation, which may confront the project during 
the course of its implementation. The meetings of s ub-Committees have since been 
completed and the recommendations are being placed before the Advisory Group. 
 
c) Further to the approval of a New Implementation Pla n by MHA in January 2015, 
whereby it has been envisaged to implement the IT F ramework sub-project directly by 
NATGRID with the help of a group of consultants and subject matter experts, in a more 
cost-effective manner and more conducive to maintai ning strategic controls compared 
to the earlier decision to engage a Project Managem ent Consultant for implementation 
of the sub-project, approval has been accorded by M HA in December 2015 for 
engagement of National Institute of Smart Governmen t (NISG) to provide to 
NATGRID 40 consultants. The detailed modalities of on-boarding the consultants are 
being finalised. 
 
4.2.9 The Committee is given to understand that NAT GRID aims to enhance the country’s 
counter-terrorism capability through sharing inform ation online and providing actionable 
intelligence.  The Committee while noting the reply  of the Ministry, justifying uneven trend 
of allocation of funds meant for NATGRID since last  financial year, hopes that New 
Implementation Plan (NIP) as envisaged would prove to be successful experiment in terms of 
cost effectiveness and conducive to maintain strategic control. Since this project is of national 
importance in terms of ensuring internal security o f this country, the Committee also 
recommends that bureaucratic red-tapism should be k ept at minimum to check procedural 
delay without compromising quality in implementatio n of this project.  Besides, the 
Government should make allocation as per requiremen t to help erecting necessary physical 
infrastructure and make NATGRID functional at the earliest.

DELHI POLICE  
4.3.1 The Delhi Police is the police force mandated  for ensuring and mannig law and order in 
the National Capital Territory of Delhi (NCT) which  is headed by Police Commissioner. The 
'Public Order', and Police in Delhi, the National C apital Territory comes under jurisdiction of 
Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA), Government of India and not under the Government of Delhi.

4.3.3 According to the Ministry, the less allocatio n in B.E. 2016-17 under Non-Plan heads may

• The payments to outside forces deployed in Delhi fo r maintaining law and order 
duties resulting in huge arrears.   
• Reduction of budget in B.E. 2016-17 will also affec t procurement of latest gadgets, 
weapons and equipments, which are necessary to modernize the Police Force.  
• Besides, less allocation will also increase the arrears on account of electricity, water 
charges under head “Office Expenses”.   
• Further, less allocation of budget in “Minor Works”  will hamper proper 
maintenance of buildings of Delhi Police which are already more than 25 years old 
and need routine maintenance. 
• Under Plan heads, the less allocation in B.E. 2016- 17 will affect completion of 
ongoing projects, starting new projects as per appr oved 12

4.3.8 The Committee observes that there is drastic reduction in allocation in B.E. 2016-17 
under plan as well as Non-Plan heads.  The Committe e also notes the details of negative 
consequences which this reduction may cause in term s of paying the salaries to deployed 
forces from outside in Delhi for maintaining law an d order, procurement of latest gadgets, 
weapons and equipments, proper maintenance of build ings of Delhi Police, completion of 
ongoing and new housing/office building projects, p urchase of land for new police 
stations/housing projects.  The Committee understan ds that these are essential components 
to run and modernise any Police Force.  In view of this, the Committee while expressing it 
deep concern over this drastic reduction, recommend s that this should be addressed at RE 
Stage otherwise this reduction would have adverse impact on functioning of Delhi Police.   
4.3.9 The Committee is not satisfied with the imple mentation status of modernization plans 
of Delhi Police during the last financial year.  Th e Committee finds that in most of the Plan 
schemes such as "Modernization Of Traffic And Commu nication Network Of Delhi Police", 
"Induction Of Latest Technology and Capacity Buildi ng", "Public Private Partnership 
Initiative, the Ministry has not even been able to spend 50% of the allocation made at RE 
2015-16 till 29 th  February, 2016.  It exposes low absorption capacit y of Delhi Police which 
might have led to low allocation by the Finance Min istry in this fiscal.  The Committee 
strongly recommends that Delhi Police should evalua te and analyze the reasons for under 
utilization of funds in its plan initiatives and dr aw an action plan to fast track completion of 
its schemes/projects without further time and cost overruns, otherwise it would be futile 
attempt projecting higher allocation.

Border Management  
4.4.1 India has 15,106.7 kms of land border and a c oast line of 7,516.6 kms including island territori es.  
The length of our land borders with 7 neighboring c ountries ranging from over 4000 kms in case of 
Bangladesh to over 100 kms with Afghanistan.  The D epartment of Border Management was created in the 
Ministry of Home Affairs in January 2004 with a vie w to provide focused attention to the issues relati ng to 
the management of the international land and coastal border, strengthening of border policing and guarding, 
creation of infrastructure such as roads, fencing a nd flood lighting of the borders and implementation  of 
Border Area Development Programme (BADP).  
  
4.4.2 According to the Ministry, for the financial year 2016-17 the Department of Border 
Management had projected Rs. 5045.70 crore against which an amount of Rs. 3777.40 crore has 
been allocated in the BE 2016-17.  The Ministry has  submitted that the Department of Border 
Management will seek more funds at RE 2016-17 stage, if required.   
 
4.4.3 The Ministry has furnished details of status of ongoing and new schemes/projects likely to 
be undertaken on priority basis under this allocati on.  The ongoing schemes pertaining to border 
management are given below in details:- 
I. Indo-Bangladesh Border Works 
: 
(i) Fencing: Out of total sanctioned length of 2468 km of fencing under Phase-II a length of 
1862 km has been completed, while work is in progre ss for 218.4 km. Work of fencing 
has been held up in a stretch of 267 km due to vari ous reasons like pending land 
acquisition, bilateral issues, etc. Balance stretch is not feasible for constructions.  
(ii) Border Roads :Out of total sanctioned length of 151 2 km of Border Roads under Phase-II, 
850.51 km roads has been completed, while work is i n progress for a length of 410 km.  
Construction work is held up in a stretch of 176.3 km due to pending land acquisition, 
bilateral issues, etc. Balance stretch is not feasible for construction.  
(iii) Floodlighting: Out of total sanctioned length of 28 40 km of floodlighting a length of 2008 
km has been completed, while work is in progress fo r a length of 345 km.  Work has been 
held up in a stretch 347 km due to pending fence wo rk.  Balance stretch is not feasible for 
construction.   
(iv) Border Out Posts (BOPs) :: Out of total sanctioned  383 BOPs construction work has been 
completed in 86 BOPs, while work is in progress in 102  BOPs.  Tendering process is 
ongoing for 136 BOPs, site is not available for 42 BOPs and rest are not feasible for 
construction.   
II. Indo-Pakistan Border Works   : 
(i) Fencing : Out of total sanctioned length of  2071.42 km fencing 1986.99 km has been 
completed, while work is in progress in 33 km. Work in 43 km in Gujarat will start soon.  
Balance fencing is not feasible.  
(ii) Border Road : Out of total sanctioned length of  47 3 km border roads, 264.7 km has 
been completed.  Work in 33 km is in progress and work in 43 km will start soon.  

55 
 
(iii) Floodlighting : Out of total sanctioned length of  2030.44 km of floodlighting, 1973.84 km 
has been completed, while work is in progress in 9.6 km.  
(iv) Border Out Posts (BOPs) :  Out of total sanctioned 126 BOPs,  65 BOPs have been 
completed.  Construction work is in progress in 22 BOPs, construction work will start in 
respect of 6 BOPs while site is not available for 2 BOPs.  Balance BOPs are not feasible.   
(v) The Government has approved a proposal for construc tion of Gaduli-Hazipir-
Santhalpur road in Gujarat for about 255 Km(123 Km new road and 132 Km to be 
upgraded) at an estimated cost of Rs.550 crore . Th e construction work of the road has 
been entrusted to the Roads and Buildings (R & B) Department of Government of Gujarat. 
The up-gradation work of 132.38 km has been complet ed in March, 2013 and the work in 
a stretch of 43 km is ongoing. 
(vi) Construction of  earthen bund, metalled road and Na ka- cum -Machan -cum- Fighting 
Bunkers  along the home side of existing  security fence in Jammu Sector.  Execution of a 
pilot project of 33.1 km  as suggested by the MoD i s being taken up first.  Land 
acquisition is under process.

:

Bihar, Uttar Pradesh and Uttarakhand is under progress.  Total estimated cost of the project 
is Rs. 3853 crore.

been completed.  However, at present, fencing work has been suspended temporarily in 
view of the protest raised by Govt of Manipur, loca l population against the fencing. Once 
this issue is resolved, the fencing work will be resumed.  
 
VI. Coastal Security 
  : 
 
a. The Ministry of Home Affairs has been implementing Coastal Security Scheme 
(CSS) in phases with a view to strengthening the in frastructure of the State Marine 
Police Force and patrolling and surveillance of coa stal areas, particularly shallow 
areas close to the coast.  The phase-II of the Sche me, is presently under 
implementation, w.e.f. 01.04.2011 with a duration of five years.   
 
b. The Phase-II of the Scheme has an outlay Rs 1579.91  crore (Rs 1154.91 crore non-
recurring and Rs 425 crore recurring). Coastal Stat es/UTs will get 131 CPS, 60 
jetties, 10 Marine Operation Centres, 150 boats, 35  Rigid Inflatable Boats (RIBs), 

56 
 
10 large vessels, 131 four wheelers and 242 motorcy cles and lump-sum grant of Rs 
15 lakh per CPS for equipment and furniture. 
 
c. A total sum of Rs. 122.60 crore has been released t o the Coastal States/UTs: 104 
CPS operationalized, land for 131 CPSs acquired 74 CPSs have been constructed 
and construction of 40 CPSs is underway, land for 5 8 jetties identified and land for 
23 jetties have been acquired. 22 jetties have been  operationalised,127 four 
wheelers and 214 two wheelers have been purchased a nd put to use. The 
procurement of 150 Interceptor boats of 12 Ton capa city, to be provided to Coastal 
States/UTs is under process.  For supply of 75 spec ial categories of boats/RIBs 
MoD has nominated Goa Shipyard Ltd. for supply of these boats.  
 
d. The Phase-II of the Scheme would come to end on 31. 03.2016. A proposal for 
seeking the approval of the Cabinet Committee on Se curity for extension of the 
period of implementation of the Phase-II of the Sch eme, for a period of 3 years, 
w.e.f. 01.04.2016 is under process.  
 
VII. Land Port Authority of India (LPAI) 
: 
a. Three ICP’s namely Attari (Punjab), Agartala (Tripu ra) and Petrapole (West 
Bengal) have been operationlized since 13.04.2012; 17.11.2013 and 12.02.2016 
respectively. 
b. Three more ICPs namely Raxaul (Bihar), Jogbani (Bihar) and Moreh (Manipur)  are 
to be operationlized during 2016-17 after their com pletion.   Development of ICP 
Dawki (Meghalaya) is also to be taken up during the financial year 2016-17. 
 
VIII. Border Area Development Programme (BADP) 
 
The Department of Border Management, Ministry of Ho me Affairs has been implementing 
a Border Area Development Programme (BADP) through the State Governments as a part of a 
comprehensive approach to the border management. Th e programme is a 100% centrally 
sponsored scheme.  Funds are provided to the States  as a non-lapsable Special Central Assistance 
(SCA) for execution of projects relating to infrast ructure, livelihood, education, health, agriculture  
and allied sectors.  Out of the total budget alloca tion of Rs. 990.00 crores during the current 
financial year 2015-16, an amount of Rs. 923.00 crore has been released to the 17border States.  
NEW SCHEMES  
4.5.1 With regard to new schemes pertaining to Bord er Management, the Ministry has informed 
that the proposal for construction of 1426.7 km lat eral and 752.48 km axial roads at an estimated 
cost of Rs.2990.87 crore in the State of Punjab and  Rajasthan along Indo-Pakistan Border is under 
consideration.  Besides, a new scheme namely Constr uction of border roads, helipad and helibases 
is under consideration in this Department in which 1718 km of roads and 21 helipads are proposed 
to be constructed in four states of Manipur, Nagala nd, Mizoram and Arunachal Pradesh along 
Indo-Myanmar Border. CPWD has submitted an enabling  estimate of Rs. 26.67 crore for 
preparation of DPRs for the project.  CPWD will com mence the DPRs work after the approval of 

57 
 
enabling estimate by Technical Committee and HLEC. The proposal has been taken up in 43 rd  
Technical Committee Meeting held on 08.03.2016. 
4.5.2  The Committee takes serious view of the fact that o nly Rs. 3777.40 crore has been 
allocated to Border Management Department against i ts projected demand of Rs. 5045.70 
crore for the financial year 2016-17.  The Committe e feels that the Ministry of Home Affairs 
should reconsider the allocation as the huge deduct ion of around Rs. 1300 crore expenditure 
over border infrastructure and management projects may not be advisable.  Keeping in view 
that the requirement of fencing, flood lighting and  border roads is of utmost importance to 
ensure proper border management and to check extern al security threats, the Committee 
strongly recommends that the Ministry of Home Affai rs should impress upon Finance 
Ministry to further enhance allocation at RE stage.  
 
4.5.3 The Committee takes note of the status of ong oing as well as new schemes/projects 
likely to be undertaken during this financial year.    The Committee also notes various 
bottlenecks coming in the way of the fencing, flood  lighting and border road projects such as 
issues in land acquisition, bilateral issues, prote sts by people residing in border areas, 
concerns of some border State Governments. The Comm ittee, in view of this, recommends 
that these bottlenecks should be dealt with by taki ng State Governments and people at large 
into confidence.  Issues involving neighboring coun tries who are opposed to fencing, flood 
lighting and border roads in any particular patch o f border areas should be taken up by the 
Ministry of Home Affairs in coordination with the M inistry of External Affairs through 
formal channels.   
 
4.5.4 The Committee notes the targets set under pha se-II of the Coastal Security Scheme 
such as construction of Coastal Police Stations, je tties, Marine Operation Centres, boats, 
Rigid Inflatable Boats (RIBs), large vessels, four wheelers and motorcycles.  The Committee 
also notes the submission of the Ministry of Home A ffairs that approval of the Cabinet 
Committee on Security for extension of the period o f implementation of the Phase-II of the 
Scheme for a period of 3 more years, w.e.f. 1 
st  April, 2016 is being sought. The Committee 
observes that the phase II of this scheme was suppo sed to be completed in the last financial 
year i.e. 2015-16, however, a lot of work is yet to be done under this scheme.   The Committee 
in view of this while being hopeful that these prop osals would be approved by CCS, strongly 
recommends that the Government should make all out efforts to complete this scheme during 
extended time period without any time and cost overrun. 
  
Border Area Development Programme (BADP) 
 
4.6.1 The main objective of the BADP is to meet the  special developmental needs and well being 
of the people living in remote and inaccessible are as situated near the international border and to 
saturate the border areas with the entire essential  infrastructure through convergence of 
Central/State/ BADP/Local schemes and participatory  approach. BADP covers 391 border blocks 
of 106 border districts in 17 States viz. Arunachal  Pradesh, Assam, Bihar, Gujarat, Himachal 
Pradesh, Jammu &Kashmir, Manipur, Meghalaya, Mizora m, Nagaland, Punjab, Rajasthan, 
Sikkim, Tripura, Uttar Pradesh, Uttarakhand and Wes t Bengal.  The BADP is supplemental in 
nature to fill the gaps. Funds under BADP are provi ded to the States as a 100% non-lapsable 

58 
 
Special Central Assistance. The schemes/works under taken under the BADP relate to construction 
of roads, bridges, safe drinking water supply, heal th, agriculture and allied activities, social secto r 
such as social infrastructure, capacity building an d skill development, Swatchhta Abhiyan, 
construction of toilets particularly for women,  ed ucation, sports activities, promotion of rural 
tourism/border tourism, etc.    
 4.6.2  The Ministry was asked about projected dema nds of the Ministry for BADP for this 
financial year and if it is adequate or not. In response, ministry has apprised that over the years, the 
development of border areas has become a priority o f the Central Government but the fund 
allocation of the BADP has been grossly inadequate to cater to the needs of the border population. 
According to the Ministry, on the other hand, the S tate Governments perceived development of 
border areas as the responsibility and priority of the Central Government and a very little effort has 
been made to fund these areas through State Plan so urces or to converge funds available under the 
CSS which led to a big gap between the pace of deve lopment in the border villages and the 
hinterland. 
4.6.3 The Ministry has further submitted that the b udgetary allocation for BADP for the financial 
year (2016-17) has been fixed as Rs. 990 crore agai nst a demand of Rs. 1800.00 crore and thus, it  
is grossly inadequate to meet the developmental nee d of the border population to arrest their 
migration from the remote border areas, in search of better lively hood in other developed areas. 
4.6.4 The BADP allocation for during the last three  years given as under: 
/square4 2013-14: Rs. 990.00  Crore 
/square4 2014-15: Rs. 800.00 Crore  (RE) 
/square4 2015-16: Rs. 990.00 Crore 
 
4.6.5 The Ministry has submitted that the border ar eas need massive funds for development to 
make border people self-reliant and to bring them a t par with other developed parts of the country 
to stop migration of people from the border areas.  
4.6.6    The allocation for Border Area Development  Programme (BADP) under Plan scheme in 
BE 2016-17 is to the tune of Rs. 990.00 crore.  Reg arding utilization of this amount, the Ministry 
has informed that as per the BADP guidelines  the b udgetary allocation shall be divided into two 
component- viz.(i) First component being  40% of to tal budgetary allocation for the eight  North-
Eastern (NE) States (including Sikkim); and (ii) re maining 60% of total allocation for the States 
other than the eight NE States on the basis of (i) Length of International border; (ii) Population of 
the Border Blocks; (iii) Area of the Border blocks (all the above three criteria shall have equal 
weightage). 15% weightage will be given to hilly /desert and Ra nn of Kutch area on account of 
difficult terrain and scarcity of resources and hig her cost of construction. The smaller States which 
have less International Border and small population  shall be given fixed allocation.  The State 
Governments shall manage the funds, within the allo cation made to the States as per the above 
formula, for the villages located within  the permi ssible distance from the international land border 
but are not in the blocks which touch the international border.  
4.6.7 The Committee notes submission of the Ministr y that Border Area Development 
Programme aims to meet the special developmental ne eds and well being of the people living 

59 
 
in remote and inaccessible areas situated near the international border through construction 
of physical and social infrastructure.  The Committ ee, however, is constrained to note that 
over the years the fund allocation of the BADP has been grossly inadequate to cater to the 
needs of the border population and the Centre is sh irking its responsibility.   It is also noted 
that the budgetary allocation for BADP for the fina ncial year (2016-17) is Rs. 990 crore 
against projected demand of Rs. 1800.00 crore which  is almost half of the projection.  The 
Committee is of the strong opinion that the develop ment of border areas should not suffer 
for want of funds and therefore recommends that the  allocation may be enhanced at RE 
stage to meet the developmental need of the populac e residing in border areas to check their 
migration from there.   The Committee also recommen ds that the share of BSF under the 
allocation meant for BADP should be increased from mere 10% to at least 40% as they are 
the people who happen to be well versed with the pr oblems faced by populace living in 
border areas and moreover they are much accessible to the people of border areas.     
Land Port Authority of India  
4.7.1 The Land Ports Authority of India (LPAI) is m andated with facilitating planning, 
construction, maintenance and management of Integra ted Check Posts (ICPs) along international 
borders.  The LPAI has been tasked with the respons ibility of putting in place systems which 
address security imperatives relating to the cross border movement of passengers, vehicles and 
goods as also developing Integrated Check Posts (IC Ps) at designated points along the 
international borders of India.  
4.7.2 These Integrated Check Posts are envisioned t o provide effective and efficient processing 
of sovereign functions such as security, immigration, customs and quarantine, while also providing 
support facilities for smooth cross-border movement of people, goods and transport. 
4.7.3 Regarding projected demands of the Ministry f or Land Port Authority of India and actual 
allocation made, the Ministry has informed that LPA I had prepared and submitted to the 
Government the Programme of Activities for the year  2016-17 and projected requirement of funds 
of ₹320.00 crore for creation of ICP’s (plan funds) How ever, against this demands, an amount of ₹ 
80.00 crore as plan funds for LPAI has been made. 
4.7.4 The Ministry was asked to furnish details of status of construction of various ICPs in the 
country alongwith targets for 2016-17.  The Ministry in its reply has made following submission :- 
• Three ICP’s namely Attari, Agartala and Petrapole h ave been operationlized since 
13.04.2012; 17.11.2013 and 12.02.2016 respectively. 
•  Four more ICPs namely Raxaul, Jogbani, Moreh and S utrakhandi are to be operationlized 
during 2016-17 after their completion.  
•   During FY 2016-17, LPAI has set a target to acqui re land/additional land for setting-up of 
ICPs at Dawki, Hilli, Changrabandha and Sunauli. 
 
4.7.5 According to the Ministry, LPAI has submitted  a draft of Action Plan for the year 2016-17 
as given below to the Department of Border Manageme nt, Ministry of Home Affairs to undertake 
various activities and targets for the year 2016-17.

Modernisation of State Police 
4.9.1 The Ministry was asked to brief the Committee  about achievement of the MPF scheme in 
terms of modernization of state police.  The Minist ry in its written reply has submitted that MPF 
Scheme is an important initiative of the Ministry o f Home Affairs, Government of India towards 
capacity building of the State Police Forces.  Acco rding to the Ministry, the main objectives of the 
scheme is to reduce the dependence of the State Gov ernment on Army and Central Police Forces.  
The focus of the scheme is to strengthen the police  infrastructure.  The Ministry has apprised that 
some of the major items of police infrastructure co vered under the scheme are police building, 
police housing, mobility, weapons, equipment, training, computerization and forensic science.  The 
Committee was informed that the State Governments formulate their State Action Plans (SAP) and 
the items to be procured and infrastructure to be d eveloped depends on the priority of the State 
Governments.  The committee was also informed that as per the MPF Scheme approved by the 
CCS for the Twelfth Plan period, the Plan funds are  for infrastructure works and Non Plan funds 
are for weapons, equipment for communication, training, forensic sciences, etc.

4.9.3 According to the Ministry, modernisation of t he State Police Forces is a continuous process 
and the effort of the Central Government has been t o meet the gap between requirement and 
availability of  funds at the disposal of the State  Govt. for modernisation of Police forces on 
regular basis. The Ministry was informed that the i mplementation of Modernisation of Police 
Force (MPF) Scheme was reviewed by the High Powered  Committee in the Ministry of Home 
Affairs with all 29 States this year and it was found that the scheme is popular and beneficial to the 
states as it has achieved its objectives.  
4.9.4 According to the Ministry, during the fourtee n years from 2000-01 to 2013-14, against a 
total amount of Rs. 13,749.82 crore released under the MPF Scheme, the States had utilized Rs. 
13,182.84 crore as on 3 
rd  March, 2016, which is 96% of the total fund releas ed. Under CCTNS, 
total amount of Rs. 825.13 crore have been released  to States/UTs upto 3 rd  March, 2016. Out of 
this, Rs. 698.43 crore have been utilized by them w hich is 85% of the total funds released. States 
are regularly advised for timely utilization of funds. 
4.9.5 The allocation on Special infrastructure for LWE areas has not been made in BE 2016-17 
like last financial year.   The Ministry was asked about rationale behind this decision.  The 
Ministry in its reply has submitted that Special In frastructure Scheme (SIS), was being 
implemented since 2008-09 in LWE affected States fo r providing central assistance to States for 
filling up critical infrastructure gaps of their sp ecial security forces, which could not be covered 
under any other existing scheme.    According to th e Ministry, during 11 th  Plan period (2008-09 to 
2011-12), Rs. 445.82 crore and during 12 th  Plan period (2013-14 and 2014-15), Rs. 122.13 cror e 
has been released to the LWE affected States under the Scheme. 
 4.9.6 The Ministry has further submitted that acce pting the recommendations of the 14 th  Finance 
Commission to enhance the share of States in the ne t proceeds of Union taxes from 32% to 42%, 
the Government has delinked the SIS from Central as sistance from 2015-16. The scheme has been 
transferred to the States for giving them sufficien t flexibility to conceive and implement 
schemes/programmes suited to their local needs, inc luding requirements to bridge the 
developmental deficit gaps , hence, no fund has bee n allocated in BE 2016-17 under the Scheme. 
The scheme was useful for training infrastructure, residential infrastructure, weaponry, vehicles 
and any other related items pertaining to the upgra dation and filling of critical gaps for Special 
Forces of LWE affected States.  
4.9.7 In view of the importance of the Scheme, the Committee was informed that MHA has 
proposed to revive this scheme as a component of Um brella Scheme namely, ‘Modernization of 
Police Forces’ of the Central Government from 2016- 17 in consultation with the Department of 
Expenditure.                                                                                                                                                
4.9.8 The Committee notes that Rs. 1753.90 crore ha s been allocated in BE 2016-17 which is 
a steep rise as compared with Rs. 662.11 crore as a llocated in RE 2015-16 under this scheme. 
The Committee also notes submission of the Ministry  that increased allocation is on account 
of consolidation of the some schemes under the Umbr ella Scheme of Modernization of Police 
Forces.  The Committee, however, understands that e ven this increased allocation is not as 
per requirement of the States.  The Committee is of  the view that the State Governments 
require large sum of allocation to modernize their police forces, to improve police 
infrastructure and to launch development initiative s in the Left Wing Extremist affected 
areas.  In view of the above, the Committee recomme nds that big enhancement should be 

66 
 
made at RE stage and share of Central Government sh ould be increased on all the projects 
under the scheme "Modernisation of Police Forces". Besides, no allocation has been made 
under the Head "Special Infrastructure Scheme for L WE Areas" which is essentially 
required for ensuring development in Left Wing Extr emism affected areas to check growth 
of naxalism and recommends that policy makers shoul d deliberate upon this issue and give 
Special Infrastructure Scheme (SIS) a go ahead.   
4.9.9 The Committee further notes that "The Mega Ci ty Policing", a sub-set of the Non-
Plan MPF Scheme was initiated in 2005-06 onwards an d hopes that this scheme will prove to 
be a catalyst in terms of establishing robust polic ing infrastructure and improving safety 
scenario in six identified mega cities.  The Commit tee hopes that the concerned State 
Governments will give priority to this scheme, cont ribute their share and there will be no 
unspent balances lying with the State Governments u nder this scheme.  With the 
implementation of this scheme, the Committee also h opes that policing in the identified mega 
cities will improve to a large extent.  
 
4.9.10 The Committee understands that MPF Scheme is  an important initiative which aims 
at capacity building of the State Police Forces and  help improve police infrastructure in the 
states.  The Committee notes that the utilization p attern of the States is improving over the 
years, however, it recommends that the Ministry sho uld pursue with state Governments 
which are lagging behind, to expedite works under this scheme in their respective States.    
 
Police Infrastructure 
 
4.10.1 The Ministry has informed that the funds all ocated under Head ‘Residential Building 
(Plan)’ for Central Armed Police Forces (BSF, CISF,  CRPF, ITBP, SSB) including NSG, and 
Assam Rifles at BE for 2016-17 is Rs.916.53 crore a s against the projected amount of Rs.1477.33 
crore.  The Ministry has further informed that the reduction of allocation of fund in BE 2016-17, 
will adversely impact the infrastructure projects of CAPFs in terms of followings: 
• The present level of housing satisfaction CAPFs is about 11.72% (as on 29.2.2016) which 
is dismally short of the recommended level of 25%. This will further decrease in view of 
the additional Battalions being raised by the Central Government. The shortage of fund will 
affect the projects for construction of residential quarters adversely.  
• In 2013, Govt. had approved construction of 21655 h ouses and 49 barracks which are 
under implementation. Recently, in Nov 2015, Govt. approved another proposal for 
construction of 13072 houses and 113 barracks for C RPF, CISF and ITBP personnel at 68 
sites at a total estimated cost of Rs.3090.98 crore . Shortage in budget allocation may 
adversely affect the construction. 
• This shortage of funds may also affect the future r aising plan as the infrastructure for the 
already raised battalions will have to be completed  before raising new battalions and 
utilizing them effectively. 
• A demand of Rs.12243.25 crore has been projected in  the Mid-Term Review of 12 th  Plan 
for capital infrastructure.

4.11.2  The Committee notes that the fund allocation for Re sidential Buildings for CAPFs, 
NSG, Assam Rifles and Delhi Police in BE 2016-17 is  very low compared to what was 
projected by the Ministry.  The Committee also note s submission of the Ministry that this 
reduction would adversely affect the infrastructure  projects of CAPFs and further reduce 
housing satisfaction level.  The Committee observes  that out of physical target of 4240 under 
Residential Building (plan) for CAPFs, NSG and Assa m Rifles only 898 could be achieved 
due to low allocation during the last financial yea r. The Committee also observes that out of 
the target of 72 projects of Delhi Police during th e 12 
th  Plan period (2012-17), only 31 
projects could be completed which is not even half of the target. Ramifications of low 
allocation is already visible in terms of slow pace  of construction activities, incompletion of 
targeted projects, delay in land purchase, etc.  Ta king into account submission of Ministry, 
the Committee strongly recommends that the Finance Ministry should look into this and an 
increase should be made as per the requirement of C APFs at RE stage. The Committee 
hopes that demand of Rs.12243.25 crore projected by  the Ministry under 12 th  Plan for 
capital infrastructure would be considered positively.   
4.11.3 The Committee takes note of the fact that th e CPWD has not yet started work on the 
construction of 80 quarters in NPA, Hyderabad and the Ministry is considering a proposal to 
increase the number of quarters for construction.  The Committee takes a serious note of all 
these developments.  First of all, the CPWD should have started construction instead 
construction work has been halted.  There seems to be something fishy in entire matter and a 
thorough probe needs to be done. The Committee also  desires that construction of 24 
quarters being done in NEPA, Shillong should be com pleted as per the target fixed.  There 
should be no time and cost overrun.

Demand No. 49 – Transfers to Union Territory Governments with Legislature 
 
 
5.1.1 The provision under this Demand is made for t ransfer of funds to Union Territories (with 
Legislature) i.e. NCT of Delhi and Puducherry in th e form of Grants and Loans for following 
purposes:- 
 
(i) Grant to meet non-plan deficit to UT of Puducherry.  
(ii) To make payment of enhanced compensation for death,  injury and damage to 
residential properties in Delhi during 1984 riots.  
(iii) Grants for Union Territories Plan Schemes. 
(iv) Loans and Advances to UT of Puducherry to cover gap  in resources under Non-
Plan.  
(v) Grants in lieu of share in Central Taxes and Duties to NCT of Delhi. 
 
5.1.2 The budget provision under the Head 'Plan' un der this Demand is decided by the 
Ministry of Finance. According to the Ministry, Non -Plan support to meet Non-Plan deficit is 
extended only to Puducherry as NCT Delhi has a surp lus balance from current revenues. The 
Ministry has apprised the Committee that Rs. 628.00  crore has been given to Puducherry in 2016-
17 to meet non-plan deficit.

5.1.16 Under Grants and Loans to meet Non-Plan gap,  NCT of Delhi has been allocated Rs. 
325.00 crore (Grants in lieu of Central Taxes and d uties to NCT of Delhi) in BE 2016-17.  Since 
NCT of Delhi contributes immensely through Central Taxes and duties the Ministry was asked 
about rationale behind such a low allocation under this Head.  The Ministry in its reply submitted 
as under:- 
"The Grant was being allocated to NCT of Delhi by M inistry of Finance from their 
Budget Head (Grant No. 37) upto financial year 2015-16. This allocation has been 
made for the first time under Grant No. 49 – Transf er to Union Territories with 
Legislature in BE 2016-17 with the budget provision  of Rs. 325.00 crore. This 
amount is being provided as an adhoc grant in lieu of Central Taxes and duties, 
since NCT of Delhi is not covered under the terms o f reference of Central Finance 
Commission. " 
5.1.17 Under transfer for UT Plans, Rs. 413.00 cror e has been allocated to NCT of Delhi in BE 
2016-17 which is reduction as against Rs. 444.89 cr ore which was allocated in RE 2015-16. The 
Ministry was asked to brief the Committee about pro jected demand of the Home Ministry in 
general and NCT of Delhi in particular under this H ead and whether this allocation was enough to 
meet the requirement of NCT of Delhi.  The Ministry  in its response apprised the Committee that 
an amount of Rs. 413.00 crore has been allocated to  UT of NCT of Delhi under Normal Central 
Assistance (Plan).   According to the Minsistry thi s is an untied fund and will be utilized by Govt. 
of NCT of Delhi for funding of Plan schemes. The Co mmittee was informed that projected 
demand of Govt. of NCT of Delhi and MHA under UT Plan was Rs. 1615.00 crore for BE 2016-17 
and since NCT of Delhi is a Union Territory with legislature and has its own Public account. Govt. 
of India is providing only Normal Central Assistanc e Plan for funding of Plan schemes of Govt. of 
NCT of Delhi.  
5.1.18 The Committee notes that under Grants and Lo ans to meet Non-Plan gap, UT of 
Puducherry has been allocated approximately half of  the amount as against projected. The 
Committee also notes the view of Puducherry Governm ent that the allocation sanctioned is 
not sufficient to meet the increase in non-plan expenditure over the years.  The Committee in 
view of this recommends that the Ministry of Home A ffairs should impress upon Finance 
Ministry to enhance allocation to UT of Puducherry under Grants and Loans to meet Non-
Plan gap as per requirement. 
5.2 Demand No. 90, 91, 92 ,93 and 94 (UTs without L egislature) 
 
5.2.1 The Administrators/Heads of the five UTs (Wit hout Legislature) have been delegated 
financial and administrative powers and based on th e ceilings fixed by the Ministry of Finance for 
‘Non-Plan’ and ‘Plan’ expenditure, the Union Territory Administrations prepare budget allocations 
for the schemes against the Ministries/Departments of the Central Government which are 
administratively concerned.  The budgetary break-up  is included in the Demands for Grants of the 
respective UTs (Grant Nos. 90-94) and presented by Ministry of Home Affairs.  Grant Nos. 90 to 
94 relate to five Union Territories which is detailed below:-  
• Grant No. 90:  UT of Andaman & Nicobar Islands  
• Grant No. 91: UT of Chandigarh  
• Grant No. 92: UT of Dadra & Nagar Haveli

5.3.3 During the meeting of the Committee held on 1 3 th  April, 2016, the Member of the 
Committee who also represents Andaman & Nicobar Isl ands in the Lok Sabha submitted before 
the Committee through his memoranda that committed liabilities of Rs. 893.46 crores additional 
plan fund should be given to the UT.  
5.3.4 The Ministry in its written reply has stated that committed liabilities for an amount of Rs. 
893.46 crores was proposed to be transferred from P lan to Non-Plan in BE 2016-17, based on the 
projection by different departments at the RE stage of 2015-16. According to the Ministry, the total 
projection under Plan Section includes the committe d liabilities and thus, the shortfall based on 
projection is for an amount of Rs. 613.67 crores on ly.   The Ministry has apprised that since, the 
provision for committed liabilities have already be en envisaged in the BE 2016-17, further 
additional demand on account of committed liabilities is not required. 

78 
 
5.3.5 The Committee strongly recommends that commit ted liabilities under Plan fund 
(whatever amount it may be) must be transferred to Non-Plan fund.   Besides, Rs. 893.46 
crore should be given as additional Plan fund for d evelopment of the UT of Andaman & 
Nicobar Islands.   
5.4 Fund for PRIs 
  
 
5.4.1 The Member also made a case for implementatio n of recommendation of 4th UT Finance 
Commission of MHA which suggests for providing fund  to PRIs for the year 2016-17 @ 25 % of 
Plan allocation and Non-plan etc.  He also submitted that additional fund should be provided to the 
PRI and PBMC to meet the expenses in respect of wor k awarded earlier.  In response, the Ministry 
of Home Affairs has submitted that the recommendation of 4 
th  Finance Commission of UT is being 
examined by the Administration. The Ministry has al so apprised the Committee that similar 
recommendation was made by the 3 rd  Finance Commission of UT also but the UT Administr ation 
has expressed its inability to allocate the funds g iven the rigidity of expenditure / committed 
liabilities.  The Ministry of Home Affairs has appr ised the Committee that it has accepted the vie 
of UT Administration in this regard.   The Ministry  has further apprised that as regards, Non-Plan 
funds, entire allocation is towards committed liabilities. 
 
5.4.2 The Committee is constrained to note that the  Government of India has not accepted 
the Reports of the 3 
rd  as well as 4 th  Finance Commission of UT.  The Committee is not re ady 
to buy the argument of the government that rigidity  of the expenditure/committed liabilities 
has made the Government unable to allocate the fund s to PRIs/Municipal Council.  The 
Committee in view of this recommends that 4 th  UT Finance Commission Report should be 
implemented in toto by the Government in PRIs/Municipal Council.  
 
5.5 Consultation with Peoples Representatives 
5.5.1 Regarding allocation of Plan and Non-Plan Fun d in consultation of 2 Adhakshya (PRI) and 
1 Chairman (MC) and MP as representing State Plan B oard, the Ministry has submitted that the 
Govt. of India, Ministry of Finance normally commun icates the budgetary ceiling for Budget 
Estimate of Non-Plan fund for next year along with the ceiling of Revised Estimates of current 
financial year during January and accordingly, the allocation is being finalized as per trend of 
expenditure and actual requirement.  The Ministry h as further submitted that budgetary ceiling for 
Budget Estimate of Plan fund for next year is commu nicated by Ministry of Finance during 
February and accordingly, allocation is being made as per requirement projected by the 
departments and taking into account the committed l iabilities/rigidity of expenditures.  The 
Ministry has apprised the Committee that by that ti me the PRIs/PBMC do not finalise their Plan 
proposal and it being a time bound exercise consult ation with the representatives of State Planning 
Board could not be made. 
5.5.2 The Committee is of the view that peoples' re presentatives should have a say in 
decision making process of UT since it does not hav e any legislative assembly where 
grievances of the people can be heard. The Committe e recommends that the UT 
Administration should consult elected representativ es while taking any policy 
decision/development measures/allocation of funds on any Head concerning the UT.  
 

79

written reply has furnished details of progress of ten years in rural roads  compared to achievement 
of Modi Government which is given in table below:-

5.6.2 The Committee takes note of the reply and fee ls that the development of rural roads 
and maintenance is not as per requirement of the UT .  The Committee is not satisfied with 
slow pace of development of rural roads and mainten ance of the same.  The Committee 
recommends that the Government should pay attention to development of rural roads to help 
improve connectivity in the UT.  
 
5.7 Improvement of Existing Roads 
 
5.7.1 On the issue of allocation of Non-Plan fund e specially on improvement of existing road, 
the Ministry has submitted that allocation of Non-P lan funds had been made as per availability of 
funds and taking into account the committed liabilities/rigidity of expenditures. 
 
5.7.2 The Committee is not satisfied with the reply  and recommends that allocation should 
be made as per requirement of the UT in terms of improvement of existing roads in the UT.   
 
5.8 Establishment of Polytechnics 
 
5.8.1 In his memoranda, the Member has also raised the issue of establishment of 2 New 
Polytechnics at Diglipur, North & Middle Andaman District and Nicobar District.  The Ministry in 
its written submission has stated that the departme nt has carried out study and recommended as 
under:- 
 
(a) North & Middle Andaman District 
 
• First starting of community college/ITI and subsequ ently upgradation to 
Polytechnic. 
• To start Polytechnic under the scheme with courses in emerging fields. 
(b) Nicobar District  
•  To strengthen the upcoming ITI at Arong, Car Nicobar with additional trades. 

80 
 
• Starting Diploma College not appear to be feasible at this stage. 
     
5.8.2 According to the Ministry, however, various o ptions for finances are being worked out 
including source of funds and final decision will be taken subsequently. 
 
5.8.3 The Committee is at pains to note that there are three districts in the UT, however, 
only one district (South Andaman) has got a polytec hnic and other two are not having any 
one polytechnic.  It was brought to the notice of t he Committee that the Government of India 
in 2008-09 with objective of skill development sanc tioned two more polytechnics in the 
districts of Car Nicobar and North Andaman district  and still it has not been opened.  The 
Committee takes serious view of this fact that year s have passed and polytechnics are yet to 
be established for want of land and fund. The Committee in view of this recommends that the 
UT Administration should provide land and fund with out any procedural delay to fast pace 
opening of two polytechnics in the UT.   
 
5.9 Establishment of Sainik School 
 
5.9.1 With regard to establishment of Sainik School  , the Ministry has informed that A & N 
Administration took up the matter of opening of Sai nik School in Andaman and Nicobar Islands 
with the Ministry of Defence, Govt. of India and  t he Ministry of Defence has requested for 
identification of suitable land. The Ministry has f urther informed that for a Sainik School of 300 
students’ capacity, 38 Acres of land and approximat ely Rs.80 Crore will be required to create 
physical infrastructure. The ministry has added tha t proposal for allotment of suitable land for 
Sainik School has already been mooted by the Directorate of Education. 
5.9.2 According to the Ministry, considering the fa ct that the Union territory has limited 
resources and is funded by the Central Government, the capital cost of establishing a Sainik School 
will be an additional burden which doesn’t seem fea sible with limited finances. The Ministry has 
submitted that  the Hon’ble Lt. Governor, A&N Islan ds, vide D.O letter dated 29 th  February, 2016, 
has requested the Hon’ble Raksha Mantri for opening  of Sainik School in the UT of A&N Islands 
by sanctioning entire capital cost of the project as a special case.   
5.9.3 The Committee is not satisfied with the submi ssion of the Ministry and recommends 
that the Ministry of Home Affairs cannot wash its hands off just by writing to the Ministry of 
Defence for   opening of Sainik School in the UT of A&N Islands with request for sanctioning 
entire capital cost of the project as a special cas e.  The Committee recommends that the 
Government should take a call on this issue and mak e allocation as per requirement to open 
up Sainik School in UT.  Besides, the Committee rec ommends that the land required for 
Sainik school which is available in the UT should be acquired for the purpose.  
5.10 Establishment of Navodaya Vidhyalaya  
 
5.10.1 On the issue of establishment of Navodaya Vi dhyalaya at South Andaman District pending 
since 2004, the Ministry has submitted as under:- 
 
"With the bifurcation of Andaman District, the Sout h Andman District is to be 
provided with a Navodaya Vidyalaya as per the polic y of GOI. Accordingly, for 

81 
 
establishment of Navodaya Vidyalaya, land measuring  10 Hect was earmarked in 
Rabindra Nagar, Little Andaman. The land was inspec ted by the representatives 
of NVS Regional office, Hyderabad and found to be n ot suitable being isolated 
location. Accordingly, an alternate land at Port Mout in South Andaman has been 
identified. Request has been made to Navodaya Vidya laya Samiti to physically 
verify the identified land to assess its suitability." 
 
5.10.2 It was brought to the notice of the Committe e that the proposal of Navodaya 
Vidhyalaya  is pending since 2004 and still nothing  is visible on ground.  The Committee 
takes serious view of this fact and recommends that  all procedural technicalities should be 
minimized and fast pace opening of the Navodaya Vid hyalaya in the South Andaman 
district.  
 
5.11 Inadequate allocation on road sectors 
 
 
5.11.1 Regarding inadequate allocation and nominal expenditure on road sectors by APWD, 3 
Tier PRIs and PBMC, the Ministry has informed that additional Requirement will be projected at 
the RE stage and Fund available with APWD is sufficient to implement PMGSY Works. 
 
5.11.2 It was brought to the notice of the Committe e by the Member representing Andaman 
& Nicobar Islands in Lok Sabha that unspent balance s are lying with APWD which was 
sanctioned long back in around 2002-03 and the UT Administration is not taking any interest 
in development of roads under PMGSY.   The Committe e would like to be apprised about 
reasons behind under expenditure in this scheme and  year-wise details pertaining to 
development of roads and expenditure under PMGSY an d other centrally sponsored 
schemes since 2002. 
 
5.12 Payment of Ex-ATI workers 
 
 
5.12.1 On the issue of Payment of Ex-ATI workers, t he Ministry has submitted that the Supreme 
Court has recently (22.02.2016) dismissed the revie w petition filed by the Commissioner Land 
Acquisition (A & N Administration.) and pronounced that the ATI union to work-out the 
workmen’s right to get the arrears including termin al benefits out of the compensation amount that 
will be determined by the Collector in respect of t he acquired property. According to the Ministry, 
in the light of the latest judgment of the Hon’ble Supreme Court, the District Authority i.e. 
Collector has to take necessary action in the matter of realization of dues of ex-workers of ATI and 
the District Authority is taking necessary action in this regard. 
 
5.12.2  The Committee recommends that the Ex-ATI wo rkers should be compensated while 
taking their issues and concerns under consideratio n at the earliest.  The Committee should 
also be apprised action taken in this regard.

5.14 Issues raised in meeting of DRSC on Home Affai rs  
5.14.1 The Hon'ble Member in his memoranda highligh ted some points raised by Members during 
deliberation in the meeting of the Committee held on 27 th  March 2015 which are as under  :- 
 
• Non- Lapsable of fund as in the case of Northern St ates where unspent balances are 
carried forward for the next year- Similar provision may be made.  
• Relax rules/ norms of 30 th  September cut off date and direct the Home Ministe r to 
take up the matter with Finance Ministry for changing its unspent date 30 th  Sept. for 
1 half of expenditure which is allocated in the budget estimate.  
• The committee recommends that high level committee in the ministry of Home 
affairs consisting public representative to monitor  the project / schemes under 
implementation in particular and development in general in UTs.  
 
5.14.2 The Ministry in its written submission has f urnished its action taken on these issues flagged 
by Committee which is as under:- 
• The issue of non-lapsable fund as in the case of No rthern States where un-spent balance is 
carried forward for the next financial year, the ma tter is being considered in the Ministry in 
respect of UTs. 
• With regard to constitution of High Level Committee  in the MHA involving public 
representative to monitor the projects/schemes unde r implementation particularly for 
developmental schemes of UTs, it is stated that the  UTs are either having HMAC or AAC 
wherein public representatives of the concerned UTs  are member and involved in 
formulation and implementation of such developmenta l issues implemented by the 
concerned UT Administrations. 
• As regards relaxation in rules/norms, with regard t o cut-off date as 30.09.2016 for 
assessment of expenditure incurred by the UT Admini strations during the current financial 
year, the matter was taken up with the Ministry of Finance to give exemption for UT of 
Andaman & Nicobar Administration and UT of Lakshadw eep Administration.  The request 
made by the Ministry was agreed to by them.  Accord ingly, the Ministry of Finance did not 
impose any cut in Plan ceiling in respect of both U Ts during the RE 2015-16, instead 
additionally Rs. 200 crores was provided at RE stag e under Plan to UT of Andaman & 
Nicobar Administration. 
5.14.3 The Committee takes note of the rely and rec ommends that the Home Ministry should 
take an early decision in regard to implementation of non-lapsable fund policy where in the 
case of North Eastern State unspent balance is carr ied forward for the next financial year, 
also for the UTs.    

83 
 
5.14.4 The Member representing Andaman and Nicobar Islands in Lok Sabha apprised the 
Committee that no meeting of HMAC or AAC has been called in the last 8 years not to say of 
inviting him in the said meetings. The Committee is  displeased to note that the Ministry of 
Home Affairs is not giving clear picture of the iss ues raised by the Member and recommends 
that the UT Administration should call such meeting s on regular basis and invite peoples' 
representatives in these meetings. 
5.14.5 The Committee takes note of the submission o f the Ministry regarding relaxation of 
cut off date for UTs of Andaman & Nicobar Islands a nd Lakshadweep and would like to be 
apprised with the decision taken in this regard in writing.   
 
5.15 Compensatory Pay to Fire Service Personnel 
 
5.15.1 With regard to view of the Committee express ed by it during its meeting held on 17 th  April 
2016, on Compensatory Pay to Fire Service Personnel , UT, Andaman & Nicobar Islands in which 
it batted for  early decision in re-installment of compensatory pay to Fire Service personnel , the 
Ministry has apprised the Committee as under:- 
   "On receipt of communication from public represe ntative and in compliance of 
directions of CAT, Kolkata Circuit Bench at Port Bl air in a case filed by Fire 
personnel of UT of A&N Administration that similarl y situated Fire personnel of 
NCT of Delhi, UT of Puducherry, State Govt. of Jamm u & Kashmir and Himachal 
Pradesh are getting the compensatory allowance, the  matter was taken up with the 
Department of Expenditure (DoE), Ministry of Financ e.  The DoE returned the 
proposal with the advice to first complete the exer cise as observed by the CAT, 
Kolkata Circuit Bench at Port Blair.  Accordingly, the State Governments/UTs 
have been requested to confirm the same and also to indicate quantum of monetary 
assistance is being provided to their Fire personne l as a kind of compensatory 
allowance. The matter is being pursued with the sta keholders with utmost sincerity 
and on receipt of their response, the matter will b e taken up with the DoE for their 
concurrence."

5.16.3 The Committee notes that the allocation to t he UT is not as per requirement especially 
on Plan side and therefore the Government should consider enhancement at RE stage. It also 
takes note of the slow pace of umpteen number of pr ojects in various sectors in the UT.  The 

84 
 
Committee is unable to understand dichotomy between higher allocation and incompletion of 
projects.  Crores of funds are being pumped in the UT but nothing substantial is visible on 
the ground in terms of social and physical infrastr ucture. The Committee strongly 
recommends that policy makers should deliberate on the slow pace of development in the UT 
of Andaman and Nicobar Islands and come out with sh ort term strategies to achieve them.  
The Committee also recommends that   the Government  should seriously look into all the 
issues flagged by the Member representing Andaman a nd Nicobar Islands in Lok Sabha and 
address them at the earliest and apprise the Committee about action taken in this regard.

5.18.5 During the meeting of the Committee held on 13 th  April, 2016, one Member of the 
Committee while expressing his dissatisfaction over  low allocation to UT of Chandigarh, raised 
following issues concerning the UT of Chandigarh 
(i) The initiatives taken or proposed to be taken to de congest traffic in the city of 
Chandigarh.  The provisions made for the same in th is budget.  The status of 
proposed metro network in the UT of Chandigarh     
(ii) Over the years employees from Punjab and Haryana wo rking on deputation in 
UT of Chandigarh has declined.   There are reports that the UT Administration 
had filled several posts which used to be occupied by deputationists from 
Punjab and Haryana earlier. The posts have been fil led by way of direct 
recruitment through the UPSC in case of Group B pos ts while those of lower 
categories were filled by the Administration itself , not leaving much scope for 

86 
 
deputationists. The reason for not maintaining the 60:40 ratio of employees 
posted on deputation in Chandigarh from Punjab and Haryana.  
(iii) The rationale behind Punjabi not being made second language in the UT of 
Chandigarh.  
 
5.18.6 The Committee notes that there is an increas e of Rs. 313.14 crore in BE 2016-17 over 
BE 2015-16.  The Committee also notes that the allo cation is not in sync with what was 
projected by the UT of Chandigarh. While noting the  effect of low allocation on projects 
under Plan Schemes, the Committee recommends that t he Ministry of Home Affairs in 
tandem with UT of Chandigarh should pursue with Fin ance Ministry to get increased 
allocation as per requirement of UT at supplementar y grant.  The Committee is of the view 
that the reduced allocation might affect projects o f social infrastructure such as Education, 
Health, Municipal Corporation, etc.   
5.18.7 The Committee also notes submission that the  low allocation under the Head 
"Health" is also insufficient for many projects to improve Health infrastructure in the UT.  
The Committee recommends that the Government should  look into this and enhanced 
allocation to help improve health facilities in the UT.  
5.18.8 The Committee notes the submission of the Me mber of Committee and recommends 
that the Ministry of Home Affairs should pay attent ion to growing crisis of traffic congestion 
in the UT of Chandigarh.  Besides, the issue of dec laring Punjabi as second language of the 
UT of Chandigarh may be considered in view of the location of the UT.    
5.18.9 The Committee understands that Chandigarh be ing the joint capital of States of 
Punjab and Haryana, is a centrally-administered uni on territory (UT) where for about four 
decades, the 60:40 quota system is in place for fil ling vacancies in the Chandigarh 
administration from Punjab and Haryana, respectivel y. The Committee recommends that 
this system should be adhered to and there should b e no violation of this arrangement 
sanctioned by Punjab Reorganization Act, 1966.   
5.18.10 The Committee notes that the budget allocat ion for the Municipal Corporation of 
Chandigarh is not as per requirement. The Committee  was apprised that the Municipal 
Corporation has made plans for various development works which will be left incomplete for 
the want of necessary budget allocation for the Mun icipal body.  The UT Administration has 
got an extra canal from Bhakra Nangal duly approved  by Punjab and Haryana 
Governments and to get this water to Chandigarh, a canal and pipeline from Kajauli water 
works are to be built at a projected cost of Rs. 25 0 crores and in view of this, the Committee 
recommends that the Municipal Corporation should be  given required amount so that the 
water problems of Chandigarh could be addressed.     
DEMAND NO. 92 (Dadra & Nagar Haveli)
 
5.19.1 The UT of Dadra and Nagar Haveli has been al located Rs. 1055.84 crore in BE 2016-17 
which is little more than Rs. 1039.18 crore as allo cated in RE 2015-16.   On being asked about 
reasons behind such a nominal increase, the Ministry has submitted as under:  

87 
 
"Union Territory of Dadra and Nagar Haveli have bee n allocated Rs. 
1039.18 crore (Rs. 907.97 crore in Plan and Rs. 131 .21 crore in Non-Plan 
on net basis) in R.E. 2015-16. The allocation under  B.E. 2015-16 has been 
Rs. 891.56 crore (Rs. 750.00 crore in Plan and Rs. 141.56 crore in Non-
Plan on net basis) which has been increased to Rs. 1039.18 crore in R.E. 
2015-16. The allocation in B.E. 2016-17 is Rs. 1055 .84 crore (Rs. 900.00 
crore in Plan and Rs. 155.84 crore in Non-Plan in n et basis) against the 
allocation of Rs. 891.56 crore in B.E. 2015-16. The  increase in Budget 
Allocation in B.E. 2016-17 is 18.43% more comparing  to the B.E. of 2015-
16, which will be utilised for meeting the additional requirement. " 
5.19.2 The Ministry was asked about projected deman d of the UT of Dadra & Nagar Haveli 
/Ministry of Home Affairs and whether the allocatio n made would be sufficient for the targeted 
initiatives during the year.  The Ministry in its r eply has informed that the projected demand for

5.19.3 The Ministry was also asked about projects t o be taken up for Energy security, Health, 
Rural Development and Housing and Urban Development  in the UT during financial year 2016-
17.  The Ministry in its reply has furnished detail s of the projects proposed to be undertaken in the

colleges and development in the area of education.

Panchyats of UT.  
(ix) Rs. 100 crore is required for sewerage system in many areas of the UT.

5.19.7 The Committee notes that the allocation to U T of Dadra and Nagar Haveli in BE 
2016-17  is not in tune with what was projected by the UT as allocation is almost half of 
projected amount. The Committee is not convinced wi th the submission of the UT 
Administration that it would try to implement the s chemes/programmes with the agreed 
allocation in B.E. 2016-17 and recommends that   th e UT should be provided with additional 
fund at RE Stage otherwise development of this UT will be hampered.

Energy security, Health, Rural Development and Housing and Urban Development in the UT 
and hopes that these schemes will be completed with out time and cost overrun and go a long 
way in development of this Union territory.   
5.19.9 The Committee takes serious view of the fact  that a meager sum of Rs. 5.97 crore has 
been allocated in BE 2016-17 for "Small and Village  Industries" which is drastic reduction 
over RE 2015-16 i.e. Rs. 11.98 crore.  The Committe e is unconvinced with the reply of the 
Ministry that the reduced allocation will not impac t the project underway as there is less 
project proposals due to slow down of industrial gr owth and recommends that the Ministry 
should give clear picture on the issue as "Small an d Village Industries" is considered to be 

90 
 
source of livelihood of many poor people and in view of the Committee higher allocation may 
increase pace of growth in the said sector.   
5.19.10 The Committee also takes note of the issues  concerning the UT of Dadra & 
Nagar Haveli flagged by Member of the Committee. Th e Committee also takes note that 
most of the issues were raised earlier and taken up  with the Ministry, however, nothing has 
reflected on ground.  The Committee strongly recomm ends that the Ministry of Home 
Affairs should look into the issues and address the m at the earliest.  The Committee would 
also like to be apprised regarding action taken in this regard.

5.21 Make Daman Smart City 
 
5.21.1 On the request of  including Daman & Diu in the smart city project,  the Ministry has

Government of India, total 100 cities were to be in cluded based on the criteria laid down by the 
Ministry. Accordingly, both the towns were proposed  before the High Powered Steering 
Committee for Smart City Mission and after evaluati on, Diu town scored more as compared to 
Daman town hence, Diu town has been included in the  first phase of the Mission.   The Ministry 
has further submitted that Daman town would be prop osed to include in the second phase of the 
Mission.

5.23 Drinking Water Facility 
 
 
5.23.1 Due to shortage of potable ground water and short availability of water from Madhuban 
Dam to Daman and Rawal Dam to Diu,  the Member requ ested for treatment of Sea water and 
supplying it to the house holds  for drinking and o ther purposes.   On this issue, the Ministry has 
apprised the Committee that with regard to Daman di strict, there is an agreement between Govt. of 
Gujarat and U.T. of Daman & Diu regarding release o f water from Madhuban Dam for following 
purposes and quantity. 
Sr. 
No. 
 
Purpose Quantity 
(MLD) 
1. Irrigation 70.13 
2. Drinking water supply 23.00 
 TOTAL 93.93 
 
5.23.2 According to the Ministry, due to industrial ization and urbanisation of Daman District, 
requirement of water for irrigation has reduced dra stically and most of the water released from 
Madhuban Dam will be utilised for drinking purpose after treatment. Accordingly, a pipeline is 
being laid to bring 64 MLD water from Madhuban Dam to Dabhel Pond and Dunetha Pond. 
Accordingly, works for installation of 3 new Water Treatment Plants (20.5 MLD at Dabhel, 12 
MLD at Magarwada and 17 MLD at Dunteha) are at vari ous stages of execution.   The Ministry 
has apprised that as on date 2 WTPs at Dabhel (16 M LD) and Magarwada (5 MLD) are in 

93 
 
operation and after commissioning of all these sche mes it is expected there will be sufficient 
potable water available for Daman District. 
 
5.23.3 In the case of Diu, according to the Ministr y, at present there is shortage of availability of 
water from Raval Dam, because of which the 4.5 MLD capacity WTP could not run to its full 
potential and 2 – 2.5 MLD water is being treated.   The Ministry has submitted that a new scheme 
with WTP of 8 MLD capacity at Kevdi with off take o f 9 MLD water from Sardar Sarovar Canal 
based project, near Una, Gir Somnath District is un der execution and in this connection, an 
agreement has been signed by UT Administration and Govt. of Gujarat to draw 9 MLD water for 
this scheme.   The Ministry is of the view that aft er commissioning of this scheme it is expected 
there will be sufficient potable water available for Diu District. 
 
5.23.4 On the request of treatment of sea water to turn it into potable water, the Ministry has 
submitted that the process of treatment of sea water will be studied and put on ground as and when 
needed.

5.26 Declaring Daman in CRZ II and Diu as an island
 
5.26.1 On the long pending demand for declaring Dam an in CRZ II and Diu as an island, the 
Ministry has submitted in writing which is reproduced below:   
"The CRZ Notification 1991 was issued by Govt. of I ndia vide Notification No. 
S.O. 114 (E). Dated 19 th  February, 1991 by Ministry of Environment & Forest s, 
New Delhi. Accordingly the Central Zone Management Plan for Daman as well as 
Diu were prepared and got approved by Govt. of Indi a and notified vide 
Notification No.DCF/DMN/200-III/96-97/739, dated 29 th  November, 1996, and 
No.DCF/DMN/200-II/96-97/740 dated 26 th  November, 1996 for Daman and Diu 
respectively. Subsequently, Govt. of India issued C RZ Notification 2011, on 6 th  
January, 2011 vide S.O. 19(E), and giving a time pe riod of 24 months to the 
states/UT to submit the Coastal Zone Management Pla n to Government for India 

94 
 
for approval. Accordingly, the draft Coastal Zone M anagement Plan for Daman & 
Diu were published in March, 2014 inviting suggesti ons and objections from 
people. Suggestions/objections were received from p eople and public hearing was 
also conducted for the purpose." 
 
5.26.2 The Ministry has further submitted that the preparation of High Tide Line map of Daman & 
Diu was entrusted to the Institute of Remote Sensin g, Chennai which was to be verified for its 
correctness by National Centre for Sustainable Coas tal Management (NCSCM), Chennai, 
however, it is learnt that NCSCM, Chennai has prepa red the HTL map of Daman and Diu afresh 
which is being finalized soon.   According to the M inistry, on receipt of the approval of High Tide 
Line Map, the proposed CZMP for Daman as well as Di u, delineating the extent of various zones 
of CRZ I, II, III etc are required to be superimpos ed on the HTL map and to be submitted to 
Government of India for according necessary approval. 
 
5.26.3 The Ministry in its submission has assured t he while finalizing the CZMP for Daman & 
Diu, the points raised by Member of Parliament, Dam n & Diu shall be taken into consideration, 
duly justifying the reason, for the special consideration of Government of India.
