5.32 Pollution in Daman Ganga River 
 
5.32.1 It was brought to the notice of the Committe e that chemically polluted and Non-treated 
water was being discharged from the companies of GI DC Vapi, Gujarat which has caused water 
Daman Ganga River polluted and loss in marine life which has affected tourism.  The Ministry was 
asked about the issue and in response it submitted that the UT Administration is concerned and 
committed to continuous improvement of water qualit y of Damanganga river and in spite of the 
fact that all the industrial effluents generated in  Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman area are 
treated in the ETP of the respective effluent gener ating units, and recycled for in house 
consumption for gardening etc of the same unit, the  UT Administration has initiated number of 
steps to further improve the quality of water of Damanganga river. 
5.32.2 According to the Ministry, the UT Administra tion is seriously concerned about the problem 
of pollution in the river Damanganga which is direc tly or indirectly affecting the population of 
Daman in general and fishing communities in particu lar. The Ministry has informed that stringent 
action is being taken by the UT administration of D aman & Diu for controlling the pollution of 
river by the industries operating in Daman. Besides , action has been initiated by the UT 
Administration for proper sewerage system to check pollution being caused by domestic effluents. 
 
5.32.3 The Ministry has further informed that the U T Administration of Daman & Diu and Dadra 
& Nagar Haveli has awarded the work to National Env ironmental Engineering Research Institute, 
Nagpur for preparation of a “Comprehensive Plan for  the abatement of Pollution in Damanganga 
River” at a cost of `1.20 Crore + applicable tax. T he study by the NEERI, Nagpur a constituent of 
Council for Scientific and Industrial Research has already commenced in March 2015 and shall be 
completed in 15 months time. According to the Ministry, the study by NEERI shall consider all the 
major industries/industrial clusters operating alon g the periphery of Daman Ganga River for the 
river stretch of approximately 35-40 Kms (from the Madhuban Daman in Gujarat to the mouth of 
Arabian Sea in Daman) and its tributaries. The Mini stry has assured that further action on the 
improvement of water quality of Damanganga river sh all be taken on receipt of the detailed report 
from NEERI. 
 
5.32.4 The Ministry has apprised the Committee that  the NEERI, Nagpur has submitted an interim 
report for study being carried out and analysis of the report for the study conducted during post 
winter and summer reflects that the physicochemical  characteristics of river Damanganga are 
almost normal upto the point of Vapi weir.  However , there is sudden change in the 
physicochemical characteristics such as colour, BOD (biological oxygen demand), COD (chemical 
oxygen demand) etc from CETP discharge points onwar ds. The COD has been observed to be 

97 
 
highest at CETP discharge point (64 mg/L during sum mer and 60 mg/L during post winter) at 
CETP discharge point. 
 
5.32.5 The Ministry has further apprised that it is  worth mentioning here that there are many 
polluting industries operating in Vapi and the issu e regarding polluted effluents being discharged 
into the river Damanganga by CETP, Vapi has been hi ghlighted on many occasions at various 
forum.  According to the Ministry, the change in co lour of the water of river Damanganga from 
Namdha village is a clear testimony of this fact an d in view of this, stringent action is required to 
be initiated by Government of Gujarat and Gujarat P ollution Control Board against the defaulting 
units of Vapi to improve the environmental status of the river Damanganga.  
5.33 Licence to Diu Airport  
5.33.1 Diu is connected to Mumbai by air, however, the Diu Airport does not possess a License 
for its use till date.   The Member apprised the Co mmittee that Director General of Civil Aviation 
(DGCA) has requested the Administration of Daman & Diu to apply for the license or surrender 
the Airport to Airport Authority of India to be maintained by them.  The Member has requested the 
UT Administration of Daman & Diu to take action on the suggestion of DGCA or else the Air 
operations may discontinue their service to Diu, wh ich is not in the interest of the UT.  He also 
requested that the Air India should operate seven f lights a week instead of four flights they operate 
from Monday to Thursday.  
5.33.2 The Ministry in its response has made follow ing submission:- 
 Aerodrome Inspection of “Diu Aerodrome” for issuing  of Aerodrome License 
was carried out by the Directorate General of Civil  Aviation (DGCA) from 19th 
to 21st July, 2011.The Action Taken Report (ATR) on  points raised by the 
inspection team is submitted to the Director (Opera tion), DGCA, Delhi on 
regular basis.  In order to comply with the observa tions in the DGCA inspection 
report, the U.T. Administration of Daman &Diu had r equested the Airport 
Authority of India (AAI) to take up work of constru ction of ATC Tower-cum-
Technical Block-cum-Fire Station, Extension of R/W by 191 mts at 2/3 end, 
provision of turning pad/Blast/RESA on both ends of the Runway & other related 
works. The UT Administration has already deposited ` 16.15 crore with the 
Airport Authority of India during the year 2015-16.  In addition to the above 
works, the Regional Executive Director (WR), AAI Mu mbai has been requested 
to carry out additional works related to preparatio n of Aerodrome Manual, 
safety management system & Standard Operation Proce dures etc. The Diu 
Airport was again inspected by the DGCA & Director of Operations (Aero Stds) 
on 28/12/2015 and further observations on ATR were conveyed to the UT 
Administration which included Re-carpeting of exist ing Runway alongwith 
providing Runway Shoulders at both sides and Taxiwa y. Necessary formalities 
are underway to hand over this work to AAI as an additional work.  
 
5.33.3 The Ministry is hopeful that with the comple tion of these works by AAI, the Diu airport 
will be in a position to satisfy the conditions imposed by DGCA for obtaining a license.

5.36 Enhancement of F.S.I 
5.36.1 The Member apprised the Committee that F.S.I  in the commercial area of Daman has been 
increased and demanded for enhancement for the same in the residential areas as well as rural area.  
The Ministry in its response has intimated the Comm ittee that recently, FSI for industrial land use 
has been enhanced as part of industrial policy decl ared by the Administration.  The Ministry has 
assured that the matter would be examined to enhance the FSI in rural areas of Daman.  
5.37 Tourism Development in the UT 
 
5.37.1 The Hon'ble Member made a case for ring road  covering the circumference of Nani Daman 
and Moti Daman to further develop tourism in Daman.   He also requested that beautifying work of 
Government Owned gardens in Daman should be allotte d only to developers of Daman.  The 
Ministry in its response has submitted that Daman district is surrounded by Arabian sea towards its 
West and Valsad district of Gujarat towards its Nor th, East and South Side. Further, Kolak river in 
North, Kalai river in South forms the boundary of Daman and separates it from Gujarat. One major 
Damanganga river divides Daman in two parts called Nani Daman and Moti Daman. 

99 
 
5.37.2 According to the Ministry, to develop touris m and to facilitate tourists to visit Daman one 
bridge each on Kolak and Kalai river was opened for  public in the year 2015. Further, 3 bridges 
are under construction and 5 bridges are planned. H aving Arabian Sea on its west and major 
establishment along with two ancient forts in Nani Daman and Moti Daman, in view f the 
Ministry, it may be difficult to construct the ring  road in Daman.   The Ministry has further 
submiteed that however, a 4-lane coastal highway ha s been constructed and bridge is planned over 
Damanganga River.   
5.37.3 The Ministry has apprised the Committee that  the works of beautification of Govt. Gardens 
are being assigned to the contractors by following codal formalities as per rules. 
5.38  Ropeway in Daman & Diu 
 
5.38.1 With regard to provision of  Rope way in Dam an (Nani Daman to Moti Daman) and at Diu 
(Potaya dada to Ganageshwar), the Ministry has appr ised the Committee that  feasibility of the 
ropeway projects in Daman & Diu shall be explored. 
5.39  Control of Heritage Sites in the UTs 
 
5.39.1 Daman & Diu are having heritage monuments (F orts, Churches etc.) which are under the 
control of Archaeological Survey of India.  The Mem ber made a case for handing them over to the 
Daman & Diu Administration.  The Ministry in its wr itten submission has stated that all 
monuments in Daman & Diu are under the control of Archaeological Survey of India, however, the 
UT Administration of Daman & Diu in consultation wi th Archaeological survey of India has taken 
up the renovation of monuments in Damn & Diu. The Ministry has further stated that the important 
monuments of Diu like Pani Kotha, Pani Bai, two chu rches has been entrusted to INTACH for 
modernization and renovation. “Manuel Maria Barbosa  du Bocage house (House of Bockage) and 
heritage walk in Fort Area of Daman has also been i nitiated with Archaeological Survey of India 
for renovation. 
5.40 Exemption of VAT on Diesel  to run Fishing Ves sels 
 
5.40.1 In Daman and Diu the fishing vessels run on Diesel which is exempted from VAT. The 
Hon'ble requested to extend the exemption for the 1 0 years.  The Ministry in its response has 
submitted that the VAT exemption for sale of High Speed Diesel for the use of Fishing boat /vessel 
has already been extended upto 31 st  December, 2017.  The Ministry has further submitte d that the 
matter for further extension would be considered on  the basis of decision take by the Government 
of India on GST. 
5.41 Tax Holiday for UT of Daman & Diu 
 
5.41.1 On the issue of extension of the tax holiday  enjoyed by the industries beyond 2017 in the 
UT, the Ministry has informed the Committee that in  the UT of Daman & Diu exemption from 
payment of Central Sales Tax is upto 31 st  December, 2017 or 15 years whichever is earlier fr om 
the date of 1 st  Scale by the Medium Industry (MSI)/Large Scale Ind ustry (LSI) registered as such 
in U.T. of Daman & Diu.  However, the UT Administra tion of Daman & Diu and Dadra & Nagar 
Haveli has already requested Govt. of India, Minist ry of Home Affairs to introduce of VAT 
incentives/VAT exemptions to new industries and pro visions of other incentives to retain the 

100 
 
existing industrial units in the UTs as well as to encourage new investment in industrial sector vide 
D.O. letter dated 30 th  November, 2013 and letter dated 14 th  October, 2015.  
5.41.2 The Ministry has also submitted that before tax concession comes to end, a robust 
Industrial Policy has been formulated so that not o nly industries find incentive to stay back but 
also new industries may be attracted in the territory.  Accordingly, UT Administration of Daman & 
Diu and DNH announced the New Industrial Policy on 22 nd  January, 2015 to ensure sustained 
industries growth through Investment Promotion Sche me – 2015 so as to further improve the 
conducive industrial climate in the UTs and to provide global competitive edge to the industries

5.46 Repealing/Amending of Portuguese Laws 
 
5.46.1    Daman & Diu was liberated in 1961 from th e Portuguese authority but till date under the 
Goad Daman & Diu (Administration), Act 1962 all the  Portuguese laws are in force in Goa, 
Daman & Diu which create difficulties in Administra tion of Justice for the members of bench and 
bars.  The Hon'ble Member requested for that the re pealing/amending to suit Indian Laws.  The 
Ministry was asked for its comments on the issue.  The UT administration in response stated that 
the status of review and repeal of Acts has been in formed to the Ministry of Home Affairs and the 
Daman & Diu being UT without legislature, the decis ion taken by the Central Government as to 
repeal or not to repeal the said Act shall be followed by the UT Administration. 
5.47 The Member also submitted a Memoranda from the  Daman Industries Association 
regarding issues pertaining to the industrial development in the UT.    
• The present sales tax benefit to industries expires /end by 31 st  March, 2017 and therefore 
numbers of Industrialists are planning to transfer or shift their units from Daman to other 
places.  It is now feared with these developments, there will be increase in unemployment 
in the district of Daman as well as surrounding are a.  The revenue by way of sales tax, 
excise etc. will be badly affected which will in affect economic and social development and 

102 
 
under the progress and growth of residents of Daman  as well as nearby area. In view of the 
above, the present benefit of sales tax for one mor e year ie. Upto 31 st  March, 2018 or till 
such time GST is applicable in the country may be extended. 
• This union territory has  development due to the in troduction of industrial incentives in 
form of sales tax and income tax exemption introduc ed two decades ago which helped 
generating employment and opportunities for various  other commercial activities.  Besides 
giving change to overall development of this UT, in dustries have contributed considerably 
to the exchequer by way of direct indirect taxes.  Not only the living standard of local 
population has seen improvement but their attitude has gone sea change to accept 
challenges of business. 
• The trade and industry has concern regarding susten ance of the growth of this UT in 
coming years for the reason that income tax exempti on was over in 2004 and sales tax 
exemption will expire by December, 2017.  Industrie s established prior year 1998 has 
reached maturity date of exemption, other will soon join.  December 2017 appears to be too 
close and GST implementation is also not clear. 
• Industries have started migrating to other states l ike Himachal Pradesh, Uttaranchal, 
Maharashtra, Gujarat etc.  As they have effectively  implemented various scheme of 
incentives. 
• This UT is net manufacturing areas and 100% Raw mat erials comes from other States and 
almost the finished goods are sold to the other sta tes.  As being small territory, this UT 
does not have the potential to market its product in its own territory like other big states and 
it has to market its goods elsewhere in the country  and this becomes unviable as  States of 
Gujarat, Maharashtra offer various subsidies like p ower subsidy, Interest subsidy on 
Capital, Industrial promotional subsidy in differen t form, technological up gradation 
subsidy.  Some of these schemes have also been impl emented by our Administration 
recently.   
• To sustain the growth of this UT it is necessary to grant Exemption from VAT and CST not 
only to new industries but also to existing industries for another Ten years. Besides, special 
concessional GST should be for the UT and Electrical Power should be in most economical 
rate. 
• Development of Transportation Hub, Housing for employees of Industries is also needed to 
support development.  
 
5.48 The Committee notes that there is under expend iture in Demand No- 93 pertaining to 
UT of Daman & Diu during the financial year 2015-16 which in view of the Committee might 
have led to reduction in allocation in BE 2016-17 c ompared to BE 2015-16.  The Committee 
also notes that the grant to this UT is also not in  consonance with projected demand as it has 
been allocated very much less than the projected on e.   The Committee recommends that 
additional fund should be allocated to the UT as per the requirement at Supplementary stage 
of Demands for Grants. 
5.49 The Committee takes serious view of the fact t hat allocation for "Transport" has been 
drastically reduced presumably due to under utilization of allocated funds which got reduced 
at RE stage in 2015-16.  The Committee recommends t hat procedural delay should be 
minimized to complete projects without further delay.   

103 
 
5.50 The Committee notes the projects and initiativ es likely to be undertaken during this 
financial year in the sectors such as Forestry and Wild Life Development, Health, Rural 
Development and Housing and Urban Development.  The  Committee hopes that these 
projects will be projected without time and cost overrun.   
5.51 The Committee takes note of the submission mad e by the Member of the Committee 
representing Daman & Diu in Lok Sabha.  The Committ ee while noting that most of the 
issues discussed above were discussed at length in the earlier reports of the Committee, is at 
pains  to note that nothing substantial has been do ne to solve these issues. The Committee, in 
view of this, strongly recommends that the Governme nt should pay attention to these issues 
and address them on priority basis.  The Committee would also like to be apprised about 
actions/initiatives taken regarding this.  
 
5.52 The Committee also finds merit in the represen tation made by Daman Industries 
Association regarding issues pertaining to the indu strial development in the UT.  The 
Committee understands that the UT of Daman & Diu being an underdeveloped UT should be 
given incentives in terms of tax holidays (sales ta x and income tax exemption) for more years 
to stop migration of industry and capital from the UT and subsequent loss of employment 
and income.  Besides,   issues of giving subsidies like power subsidy, Interest subsidy on 
Capital, Industrial promotional subsidy in differen t form, technological up gradation 
subsidy, should be considered in the interest of the development of the UT.

5.53.7 During meeting of the Committee held on 13th  April, 2016 to discuss demands for grants 
of the Ministry of Home Affairs, a Member of the Co mmittee raised rationale behind a 15 year 
perspective plan for acquiring 9 ships for UT of Lakshadweep.   He further added as under:-  
"..  Passenger ship connectivity to the mainland with Lakshadweep is a perennial 
problem, like it is a problem with Andaman.  The Sh ipping Corporation of India 
has ships, which were examined on the earlier occas ion, which are completely 
defunct.  There are perennial problems.  You have n ot been able to persuade the 
Shipping Corporation of India to build new ships fo r ferrying passengers.   You 
are now saying that you will acquire two 800 MT mul tipurpose cargo ships.  But 
so far as passenger movement is concerned, please understand that there is a big 
problem involved in terms of health.  How do you li ft them in the case of 
emergencies?  There is a perennial problem.  For th at  you have a 15 year 
perspective plan.   But isn't that something that m erits an immediate attention?  I 
am talking of a larger issue.  In Lakshadweep, Bang aram has been closed down.  
Kabarati   is the only place where you can have some tourist traffic and tourism 
has been recognised down decades as one of the beig est sources of income and 
employment and occupation for the people of Islands .  But I see nothing here 
with any focus on development of tourism in Lakshadweep. Now Maldives claims 
Lakshadweep Island as part of their archipelago even today.   They say India has 
occupied our northern part.     Leave that aside.  How do they develop their 
tourism in Maldives, same Islands, same archipelago ?  We can understand if 
there are security concerns.  We can understand if there is something else.   This 
is an issue that has been before the Parliamentary Committees for decades.  Why 
have  we not been able to develop   what is being d one by Male?   From what 
you have allocated here, I do not see any focus on tourism and the potential 
tourism has for the Islands, Andaman and Lakshadwee p.    This is a perennial 
concern.  What is the problem?  We would like to un derstand it.   Please 
understand, if we understand it, we will be able to help you.    
5.53.8 The Administrator while replying submitted a s under: 
                   "From the years 2000-2015, we ha ve a 15 year perspective plan for shipping, 
under which we acquired about 23 vessels, in the la st 15 years, which included 
three 150 high speed ferry vessels, 100 MT barge, o ne LPG cylinder carrier, two 
trucks, eight barges,  two 250 passenger vessels,  one 400 passenger vessel, etc.   
In fact, we exceeded the target of the perspective plan.   With the result, in the 
last 15 years, the passenger carrying capacity has been more than doubled.  The 
same applies to the cargo carrying capacity also.   Now in the next 15 years, we 
plan to acquire nine more ships and vessels. There has been significant 
improvement in connectivity.  It is not ideal, but it has improved significantly.   
On the issue of tourism, we have got tourism infras tructure in Kadmat, Minicoy, 
Kabarati, Bangaram and in one more Island. Bangaram  Island has been closed 
for the last five years because of litigation.  But  we have won this case last 
month.   We propose  to reopen Bangaram.  This will be a major boost to tourism 
in Bangaram Island.  We have also made our tourism policy in the month of 
February after consultations with the local represe ntatives.   The policy has 

107 
 
come out.  We plan to further increase tourism by p rojecting Lakshadweep as a 
driving centre, as a centre for niche tourism.  It is not only Government 
investment;  we are looking for private investment and PPP projects also. .... ...  
So, tourism will get a boost. We also plan to sign a Joint Agreement with Kerala 
tourism to market Lakshadweep and Kerala together."

cable for BSNL connectivity and status of bandwidth expansion? 
• Like, the Canning Factory in Minicoy was establishe d in 1969.  During the same time, 
Maldives also established a canning factory. Their production capacity is one lakh cans per 
day, but we are stuck with 800 cans. 
• The UT has potential in terms of coconut and fisher ies which can bring revenue and 
employment generation for the people.  What steps are being taken in this regard? 
 
5.53.10 The Member representing Lakshadweep in Lok Sabha brought to the notice of the 
Committee about the issue of drastic reduction in t he availability of rice under the PDS following 
the wrong calculation by a few officials of the ave rage annual off-take of rice for the last 5-6 years  
under the normal targetted group after the implemen tation of the National Food Security Act 

108 
 
(NFSA) and thus pushing the common people to live i n semi starvation conditions on the islands. 
As per the NFSA, two-third of the total population of a State/UT has to be given the rice at a 
highly subsidized rate. However, the Lakshadweep be ing categorised as Scheduled Tribes region 
and the entire area being rural, its entire populat ion should be covered under the scheme. Further, 
other States procure the deficit quantity from the open market, and make it available to the 
population at affordable price. This can be done in  the case of Lakshadweep either by making 
additional provision in the Plan fund or by incorpo rating a separate component towards state 
subsidy to take care of the additional cost in proc uring and transporting rice to Lakshadweep. The 
additional amount required will be around Rs. 3.00 crore per annum. 
 
5.53.11 The Committee notes that the allocation to the UT is not in tune with 
projections made.  The Committee also takes note th at the allocation for health and 
Transport is also very less. In view of the very po or health infrastructure in the UT where 
people are forced to visit the state of Kerala duri ng emergency health conditions which are 
also a double blow as this UT has very poor connect ivity with mainland, the Committee 
recommends that there should be large enhancement m ade at RE stage for improving health 
infrastructure and transport (connectivity) in the UT.  Besides, facility of air ambulances 
may be made at concessional rates for people belonging to UT. 
5.53.12 The Committee also takes note of the major projects, schemes, initiatives, etc. 
proposed to be undertaken during the year 2016-17 u nder the health, education, 
agricultural, energy and allied sectors and recomme nds that all these works should be 
completed in a time bound manner.   Procedural wran gles should be avoided to fast pace 
these works. 
5.53.13 The Committee is in agreement with submissi on of the Member of the 
Committee that connectivity to the mainland with UT  of Lakshadweep is a perennial 
problem.  The Committee recommends that the Shippin g Corporation of India should 
acquire new ships for ferrying passengers as per re quirement of the UT.  The Committee 
also recommends that development of tourism sector should be given priority on the lines of 
Maldives which has same topography and natural beau ty to help generate income, 
employment and investment in the UT.   
 
5.53.14 The Committee notes the various issues flagged by M embers of the Committee 
which pertain to development of social and physical  infrastructure in the UT of 
Lakshadweep and recommends that the Ministry may lo ok into all these issues and apprised 
the Committee of the action taken.  
5.53.15 The Committee is aware that none of the ite ms like paddy, wheat or coarse 
grains are grown in Lakshadweep, though the rice is  a staple diet of the islanders. The entire 
requirement of rice has to be transported from the mainland to the Island. The Committee is 
also aware that the islanders can not afford to buy  the rice at the market prices as they are 
mainly rural and tribals. The Committee therefore r ecommends that an additional amount 
of Rs. 3 crore per annum should be provided to the Lakshadweep to meet this contingency.   

109 
 
CHAPTER - VI 
PATHANKOT AIRBASE ATTACK  
 
BACKGROUND  
 
6.1.1 On the 2 nd  January 2016, a heavily armed terror group attacke d the Pathankot Air Force 
Station, located in the State of Punjab which is part of the Western Air Command of the Indian Air 
Force. In the counter terror operation by Indian se curity agencies, four attackers and two security 
forces personnel were killed and five more security personnel died of injuries.    
6.1.2 Furnishing the details of how the attack unfo lded, the Ministry has submitted in writing 
which is as under:- 
"In this background, in the early morning of 1 st  January, 2016 at around 03.23 a.m., 
the Police Control Room, Gurdaspur received information from Shri Salvinder Singh, 
an Addl. SP rank officer of the Punjab Police poste d in 75 th  Battalion of Punjab 
Armed Police, that some unidentified persons in mil itary uniforms had hijacked his 
vehicle and kidnapped him along with his friend, Sh ri Rajesh Verma, and orderly, 
Shri Madan Gopal from Village Kohlian in the area o f PS Narot Jaimal Singh, 
District Pathankot, and subsequently he and his ord erly were thrown out of the 
vehicle near Gulpur Simbli in the area of PS Kanwan . This information was passed 
on to Pathankot Control Room of Punjab Police by th e Gurdaspur Control Room of 
Punjab Police. A red alert was sounded by the Punja b Police in Pathankot District 
and the adjoining Gurdaspur District after the rece ipt of this information. 
Subsequently, Shri Rajesh Verma was traced to Amand eep Hospital, Pathankot, with 
severe injuries inflicted on him by the abductors, who had abandoned him in Village 
Bellian, PS Sadar Pathankot. The Punjab Police laun ched a search for the vehicle of 
Shri Salvinder Singh, which was found abandoned in the fields of Village Tajpur in 
the area of PS Sadar, Pathankot.During the examinat ion of Shri Rajesh Verma by the 
police, it was disclosed that the abductors were ta lking among themselves about 
attacking some defence establishment. It was also disclosed that two mobile phones of 
Shri Salvinder Singh and one mobile phone of Shri R ajesh Verma were also robbed 
by the abductors. Based on this information, the of ficers of various agencies, 
including the Intelligence Bureau, Military Intelli gence, Punjab Police, etc., had 
arrived at the conclusion that the abductors are te rrorists, planning an attack on a 
military establishment." 
 
6.1.3 During the meeting of the Committee held on 5
th  January, 2016, the Chairman asked the 
Home Secretary to apprise the Committee, in the fir st instance with the latest status on the terror 
attack in Pathankot. The Home Secretary then appris ing the Committee on the happenings in 
Pathankot, informed the Committee that while the te rrorists had been neutralized, the combing 
operations were going on to sanitize the complete a rea.   The submission of Home Secretary is as 
follows:-  
  

110 
 
 " ................, Sir, basically, some terrorist s, who are still to be identified by name or 
by identity, hijacked a vehicle in Punjab.  In all probability, they hijacked a vehicle in 
which a driver was killed.  It was an Innova vehicl e.  Subsequently, they hijacked a 
private vehicle of one SP in Gurdaspur, who was rep ortedly coming back from 
ziyarat-e-dargah.  They travelled towards Pathankot, abandoned the vehicle and then 
they seemed to have entered the Air Force base at Pathankot injuring the friend of the 
SP in his throat but he survived that.  He is recov ering in a hospital.  On 2nd 
January, at 3.30 a.m. firing was started upon by th em.  We have certainly neutralised 
four terrorists and two more, who holed up in a mes s building, have in all probability 
been neutralised because there is no firing from th at building for the last many hours 
since yesterday.  But the rubble is being cleared a nd once the body or body parts are 
discovered, at that point of time, we will be able to say with certainty that they have 
been neutralised.  Combing operations are going on in the Air Force Station at 
Pathankot.   As it is a huge campus – I am told wit h a perimeter wall of 24 kilometres 
– and there is a lot of growth of shrubs and trees in that area, so that is being 
carefully combed to ascertain that no other persons  are hiding and the area is 
declared sanitised.  So, that is the situation today.  The investigation has been handed 
over to the National Investigation Agency which wil l investigate into the entire chain 
of events and then tell us as to what exactly happened." 
6.1.4 During its study visit to western border area s in Punjab and Rajasthan, the Committee 
visited Pathankot Airbase On 11 th  February, 2016.  The Committee was first briefed by the Airbase 
officials on the minute details of terrorist attack  that took place on the air base and the consequent  
operation that was conducted to neutralize them thr ough a powerpoint presentation.  Thereafter, 
the Committee visited the actual sites within the Airbase premises relating to this attack.  
OPERATION TO NEUTRALISE TERRORISTS  
6.2.1 The Ministry was asked about  action taken by the Central Government on the intel ligence 
inputs received.  The Ministry in response apprised  the Committee that the information regarding 
attack was shared by the Punjab Police with the cen tral agencies and the local Army and Air Force 
officials and thereafter, the interception of the m obile numbers robbed by the terrorists disclosed 
that they were planning an attack on a defence esta blishment and, accordingly, security was put on 
high alert in the nearby defence establishments. 
6.2.2 According to the Ministry, on the basis of th e above information, a meeting of senior 
officers was convened by the Central Government and  consequent to the meeting, the Army Units 
were mobilized, Air Force Commando Garuds were flow n along with Air Officer Commander in 
Chief, Western Command and the NSG was airlifted to  the most possible target of terror attack, 
the Air Force Station, Pathankot, by the evening of 1 
st  January, 2016.   The Ministry has added that 
as a precautionary measure, high value air assets w ere moved away from the Airbase and 
subsequently, Army also inducted Special Forces (1 Para) Bomb Disposal Units and other 
specialized equipments.   
 
6.2.3 Countering the allegation of lack of coordina tion between different police and security 
forces before and during the Pathankot terror attac k as reported in the media, the Ministry has 
submitted that due to the intelligence inputs made available consequent to the abduction of Shri 
Salvinder Singh and the interception of communicati on between the terrorists and their handlers, a 

111 
 
well planned, well-coordinated anti-terrorist operation was launched by the security forces and due 
to  real time mobilization and deployment, all the terrorists involved in the attack were neutralized 
without any loss of assets of the Indian Air force,  which was the main target of attack by the 
terrorists.  There was clear chain of command and control during the operation. 
6.2.4 The Ministry was asked to provide a timeline of sequence of events before and during the 
attack w.r.t.  infiltration, entry into airbase, at tack and damage by the terrorists and operation to 
neutralise them.  The Ministry in its response has stated that this aspect is under investigation by 
the NIA. 
6.2.5 There were reports that some of the terrorist s/infiltrators who were not neutralised managed 
to escape after the Pathankot attack.  The Ministry  was asked about facts in this regard.  The 
Ministry has informed the Committee that all the te rrorists involved in the Pathankot airbase terror 
attack were neutralized by the security forces.  
6.2.6 First of all, the Committee is unable to unde rstand that in spite of terror alert 
sounded well in advance, how terrorists managed to breach the high-security air base and 
subsequently made attack.  The Committee is constra ined to note that despite concrete and 
credible intelligence inputs received from abducted and released Pathankot’s Superintendent 
of Police (SP) and his friend and through intercept ion of communication between terrorists 
and their handlers by the terrorists disclosing that they were planning an attack on a defence 
establishment, the security agencies of our country  were so ill-prepared 
to anticipate threats 
in time and counter them swiftly and decisively. 
6.2.7 The Committee feels that something is serious ly wrong with our counter-terror 
security establishment. Despite the fencing, floodl ighting and patrolling by BSF personnel, 
Pakistani terrorists managed to sneak into India fr om across the border.  The Committee 
understands that  in this attack, the role of Punja b Police is also very questionable and 
suspicious as even after abduction of Punjab Police  SP, the Punjab Police took longtime in 
arriving at the conclusion that their abduction was  not just a criminal robbery but it was 
going to be serious national security threat. The C ommittee is unable to understand why the 
terrorists let the SP and his friend off, which sho uld be thoroughly examined by NIA.  
Besides, the Committee is of the view that the role  of narco-syndicate active in border areas 
of Punjab should also be investigated as the terror ists might have taken help of channels or 
networks used by smugglers to infiltrate the border, shelter and  carry out terror attack. 
6.2.8 The Committee also recommends that in view of  the series of attacks from across the 
international border in this area, the Government o f India should pay attention to effectively 
sealing the border through enhanced patrolling, fencing, flood lighting, etc.  
6.2.9 The Committee during its visit found that air base’s security cover was not robust and 
it had a poorly guarded perimeter wall.  The perime ter wall of airbase was not having   
patrolling road around it. The Committee also found growth of long shrubs and trees in the 
premises which might have helped terrorists in hidi ng and making difficult for security 
forces in combing out these terrorists.  The Commit tee recommends that the Pathankot 
Airbase which is very close to border should be dec lared high security zone and should be 

112 
 
properly secured through round the clock patrolling and making it out of bounds for general 
people residing around the airbase. 
FOREIGN HAND BEHIND THE ATTACK 
 
6.3.1 There were speculations of terrorist attack o n Pathankot Air Force base sponsored by 
elements in Pakistan.  The Ministry was asked about authenticity of this Report. The Ministry in its 
written reply informed the Committee that Indian In telligence agencies have been receiving inputs 
indicating efforts by terrorist outfit, Jaish-e-Moh ammad, led by Maulana Masood Azhar to 
infiltrate into India from Pakistan territory to ca rry out terrorist attack on Defence / Police 
establishments in India and such intelligence input s were shared with all the stakeholders 
concerned and there was a heightened security alert.   
6.3.2 In view of the Committee, there cannot be no two opinion on the fact that Pakistan 
based Jaish-e-Mohammad terror group  was behind this ghastly terror attack which was 
established through interception of calls between t errorists and their handlers based in 
Pakistan, thanks to their use of robbed mobile phones from Pathankot SP  and his friend.  
Besides, weapons, ammunitions and many items found in posses sion of killed terrorists are 
also said to be having 'Made in Pakistan' marking.  
6.3.3 In view of the ease with which the terrorists  sneaked into our side from Pakistan, the 
Committee understands that the attack cannot take p lace without active support from  
security and intelligence agencies of Pakistan as  four  armed men cannot easily pass through 
heavy Pakistani defence establishment situated  near border area.  
INVESTIGATION  
6.4.1 On the question of progress made in the inves tigation of the incident of Pathankot Airbase 
Terrorist Attack, the Ministry has submitted that t he Central Government has handed over the 
investigation of this case to the National Investig ation Agency.  According to the Ministry, the 
initial investigation has disclosed that the terror ists belonged to the Jaish-e-Mohammad terrorist 
outfit based in Pakistan and there is also evidence  on record that this terrorist attack was planned 
by the Jaish-e-Mohammad chief, Maulana Masood Azhar  and others. The Ministry has further 
submitted that investigation is still in progress i n this case and the Government of India has shared 
the relevant information about involvement of Pak nationals with the Pakistan Government.  
 
6.4.2 Regarding seeking help of Pakistan into inves tigation of this terror attack,  the Ministry 
apprised the Committee that a Joint Investigation T eam (JIT) from Pakistan come to India on 28 th  
March, 2016 to collect, review and document physica l evidences and to interview key witnesses 
and victims through the National Investigation Agen cy with regard to the Pathankot Airbase terror 
attack. According to the Ministry, they will also s hare the details of investigation done in Pakistan 
regarding the Pathankot incident. 
6.4.2 The Committee notes the submission of the Min istry that the NIA is handling the 
investigation of this case and terrorists belonged to the Jaish-e-Mohammad terrorist outfit 
based in Pakistan has been found to be behind this attack but the Committee would like to be 

113 
 
apprised what made the Government of India to seek help of Pakistan into investigation of 
this terror attack and invite a Joint Investigation Team (JIT) from Pakistan to India.  
 
PREVENTING MEASURE FOR FUTURE ATTACKS  
 
6.5.1 On being asked about preventive measures take n or proposed to be taken to check such 
incidents in future, the Ministry in its response h as stated that in order to counter terror activitie s, 
there exists close and effective coordination betwe en intelligence and security agencies at the 
Centre and the State levels.  The Ministry has furt her stated that The Multi Agency Centre (MAC) 
has been strengthened and re-organized to enable it  to function on 24x7 basis for real time 
collation and sharing of intelligence with other in telligence agencies and States, which ensures 
seamless flow of information between the State and the Central agencies.  Further, The Security 
Audit of all security related establishments / infr astructure has been carried out by the 
organizations / agencies concerned to assess physic al security measures and upgrade it, wherever 
necessary.  
6.5.2 According to the Ministry, apart from the abo ve, to counter the problem of cross-border 
infiltration and to enhance security in the border areas, the Government of India, in coordination 
with the State Governments concerned, has adopted a  multi-pronged approach, which includes, 
inter-alia, strengthening of border management thro ugh multi-tiered deployment along 
international borders/Line of Control and infiltrat ion routes, construction of border fencing, 
improved use of technology, better intelligence and  operational coordination, synergizing 
intelligence flow to check infiltration and proactive action against terrorists within the country. 
6.5.3 The Ministry has also furnished details of st eps taken by BSF to strengthen the security 
measures and to counter the cross border infiltration in Punjab border areas which is as under: 
 
• In order to strengthen border domination, one more Battalion has been inducted in 
Gurdaspur sector on Punjab Border. 
 
• One pilot project for deploying technological solut ions in 2 patches of 5-6 kilometers of 
riverine gaps and sensitive areas of Jammu Frontier has been sanctioned. 
 
• Introduction of Force Multipliers and Hi-tech surveillance equipments to reduce stress level 
of troops and enhance the surveillance border. 
 
• Upgradation of Intelligence Network and coordination with sister agencies. 
 
• Conduct of special operations along the Border and in-depth areas. 
 
• Vulnerability mapping of Border Outposts (BoPs) alo ng Indo Pak Border are being 
reviewed from time to time. 
 
• Four Pilot Projects of approx 30-40 kilometers each  in different terrains and sensitive 
riverine gaps in Jammu, Punjab, Gujarat and West Be ngal have been approved by the 
Government. 
 

114 
 
• Forty five more laser / IR Intrusion detection syst em are being fabricated and being 
installed in Punjab (eight have been fabricated and installed). 
 
• Twenty six Trg coys are being deployed on Border for better domination. 
 
6.5.4 The Committee notes the various measures take n by the Government of India to 
effectively counter future terror attacks. The Comm ittee is of the view that only security 
establishment/infrastructure in place will not do b ut there should be proper intelligence 
gathering, timely sharing of inputs between various  security agencies in real time which will 
help anticipate and launch counter terror operation s. The Committee hopes that the security 
agencies should learn lessons from terror attacks of past to avoid mistakes in future. 
 
 
  

115 
 
OBSERVATIONS/CONCLUSIONS AND RECOMMENDATIONS OF THE COMMITTEE 
AT A GLANCE  
OVERALL ASSESSMENT OF DEMANDS FOR GRANTS  
 The Committee notes that there is an increase of 1 2.15% over BE 2015-16 and 9.84% 
over RE 2015-16 to the Ministry of Home Affairs BE 2016-17.    The Committee apprehends 
that this nominal increase would just meet the infl ationary pressures. The Committee 
recommends that the Ministry after making a thoroug h scrutiny of its needs to run the 
projects in hand and projects proposed, may approac h the Ministry of Finance for 
enhancing allocation at RE stage, otherwise ongoing schemes and projects would be seriously 
affected.   Besides, currently launched projects would also be adversely affected.   
(Para 2.6) 
 The Committee is in agreement with the view submit ted by the Ministry of Home 
Affairs that the schemes to be included in the Cent rally Sponsored umbrella Scheme called 
"National Scheme for Modernization of Police and ot her forces" are of National Security 
importance and are vital for strengthening of inter nal security architecture in the country 
and continuation of support of the Central Governme nt to these schemes is critical.  The 
Committee is hopeful that the proposal of Home Mini stry for inclusion of SIS, SRE Scheme 
in umbrella scheme will be considered as these sche mes are vital for Left Wing Extremism 
affected and other troubled regions/states of the country. 
(Para 2.12) 
 The Committee is not ready to buy the argument tha t merely by enhancing the share 
of the  States in the net proceeds of Union taxes f rom 32% to 42%, the Central Government 
can justify delinking the SIS from Central assistan ce.   Besides, the Committee is of the view 
that increasing of share of the States in the  divisible pool of Union taxes to 42 percent from 
32 percent would not yield the desired results as due to the imposition of various types of cess 
and surcharge in the  BE 2016-17 such as Krishi Kalyan cess, Education cess, Swachh Bharat 
cess, Clean Energy cess etc. which go directly to t he centre's coffer not to the States, states’ 
share in the divisible pool is likely to go down in stead of 42% as claimed.  The Committee 
accordingly proposes that the decision on modality of the implementation of the Umbrella 
scheme may be devolved to the States in terms of sufficien t flexibility to conceive and 
implement scheme/programmes suited to their local n eeds but financial support from the 
Centre should continue as States particularly backw ard states have their own social sector 
commitments. 
(Para 2.13) 
 The Committee notes the overall security situation  in the country particularly in the 
North-Eastern States and Left Wing Extremism (LWE) affected States and the efforts to 
bring ultras from North East to negotiating table.  The Committee is of the view that the 
Ministry should enhance its efforts to bring these separatist forces to the main stream 
through dialogue and negotiation.   
(Para 2.15) 

116 
 
 The Committee simultaneously expresses its anguish  on the grievous breach of 
security in Pathankot and Gurdaspur (Punjab) and fe els that these incidents put a question 
mark on our security preparedness and reflects of g aps in our intelligence network.  The 
Committee observes that these incidents put shadow on all other achievements made on the 
internal security front and hence impresses upon th e Ministry to take all steps to see that 
such incidents are not repeated in future.      
(Para 2.16) 
 The Committee notes that the procedure for Securit y Clearance of proposals relating 
to Defence/ Infrastructure Projects   and Maintenan ce Policy for the Border Infrastructure 
(i.e. Border Roads, Flood Lights, Border Fencing an d Border Outposts) has been finalized.  
The Committee would like to be apprised of the same.   
(Para 2.17) 
 The Committee also notes that the Government has a pproved 33% reservation for 
women in direct recruitment for Non-Gazetted posts from Constable to Sub-Inspector in the 
CAPFs & police forces of all the UTs including Delh i Police.  The Committee hopes that 
assured 33% recruitment of women in these forces wi ll instill confidence among women folk 
and bridge gender divide prevalent in the society.  It should be implemented at the earliest. 
(Para 2.18) 
 The Committee notes that the 78% (Approx 12000) Po lice stations are registering 
100% FIR and an advisory to ensure no discrimination in registering FIRs was issued on 12 th  
August, 2015. However, the Committee doubts this cl aim and therefore, would like to know 
the basis used to arrive at this claim that 78% (Ap prox 12000) Police stations are registering 
100% FIR.  However, efforts should be continued to achieve this in all police stations.  
(Para 2.19) 
 The Committee is happy to note that various relaxa tions for long-term visa (LTV) 
holders of Pakistani minorities have been approved which include the facilitation for studies 
of their children, visit to additional places and e mployment in private sector, easy renewal of 
LTVs and renewal for prolonged period. The Committe e is hopeful that this relaxation for 
Pakistani minorities residing in India will help in their rehabilitation.   
(Para 2.20) 
          The Committee notes that the prime focus area of the Central Government on the 
Internal Security front will be countering terroris m in Jammu & Kashmir, North Eastern 
Areas, Left Wing Extremism affected States and the hinterland of the Country.  The 
Committee also notes that the Government of India i n tandem with the State Government of 
Jammu and Kashmir have adopted a multi-pronged appr oach to check cross border 
infiltration, promote economic growth, provide reli ef to victims and strengthen the State 
Government apparatus. 
(Para 2.23) 
 

117 
 
       The Committee also notes that the Government  proposes to adopt multi-pronged 
strategy to deal with extremists/separatists of Nor th East Region.  The Committee is of the 
view that the Government should start dialogue with  these groups and address 
sympathetically their grievances within the frame w ork of the Constitution of India with an 
aim to bring them into the main stream.  The multip ronged strategy should include this 
aspect also.  
(Para 2.24) 
          The Committee also notes that the priority of Central Government in dealing with Left 
Wing Extremism (LWE) is development, ensuring right s and entitlements of local 
communities and public perception management. The C ommittee is of the view that 
development measures should have precedence over se curity measures while dealing with 
Left Wing Extremists through security measures cannot be ignored.    
(Para 2.25) 
  The Committee is displeased to note the under exp enditure in respect of many of the plan 
schemes of the Ministry of Home Affairs consistentl y during the years 2012-16 (as on 29 th  
February, 2016).   The main concern of the Committe e is that during the last four financial 
years, the Ministry has not even been able to spend  the reduced allocation at RE Stage in 
several schemes which is an indicator of under-perf ormances.  The Committee notes that 
under many schemes/projects like infrastructure for  CAPFs or for Border Management 
where there are still huge gaps in what is desired and what is existing.  Moreover, the funds 
available have not been optimally utilized.  The Mi nistry needs to gear up its machinery to 
analyze what is the cause of this consistent under- utilisation and take adequate measure to 
plug the gaps for achieving the desired objectives and goals.  The Committee would like to be 
apprised about the steps to strengthen the absorpti on capacity of various schemes and 
agencies. Besides, the Committee has apprehension t hat this underutilization may prove to 
be a valid ground for further reduction in allocation during future financial allocations.   The 
Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry of Home Affairs should pay attention 
to restructuring its implementation agencies so that the schemes /projects could be completed 
without time and cost overrun.    
(Para 2.27) 
 The Committee takes note of the various reason cit ed for decrease of Rs. 237.20 crore 
in RE 2015-16 over BE 2015-16 under 'Non-Plan' and expresses its displeasure that non 
finalization of proposal under various Schemes of N ational Disaster Response Force, non 
finalization of proposal of shifting of campus of N ational Fire Service college, Nagpur and 
non finalization of rehabilitation package of displ aced Kashmiri Migrants which included 
providing 3000 Government Jobs to Kashmiri Migrants  and construction of transit 
accommodation in the Valley are some of the reasons  for this decrease.  In view of the above, 
the Committee recommends that the Ministry should d well into the reasons for these delays 
in many important schemes/projects under the Head a nd put its machinery into gear to fast 
pace finalization of these schemes/packages at the earliest. 
(Para 3.1.6) 

118 
 
   The Committee takes a serious view of the under expenditure under this grant during 
the year 2015-16.  The Committee is constrained to note that the Ministry was allocated Rs. 
1570 crore under Demand No. 53 (which is now known as Demand No. 46) which was 
increased to 1751.16 crore at RE stage in the year 2015-16 but the Ministry has been able to 
spend only Rs. 1303.17 crore at the fag end of the last fiscal.  It reflects low absorption 
capacity of the Ministry under the scheme and will weaken its case before the Finance 
Ministry while arguing for augmentation in allocation.    
(Para 3.1.8) 
 The Committee observes that against projected dema nd of Rs. 5003.97 crore under 
Demand No. 46 only Rs. 4630.90 crore (Rs. 1250.00 c rore under Plan and Rs. 3380.90 crore 
Non-Plan) has been allocated for the year 2016-17.  The Committee also observes that there 
is a shortfall in major Heads such as Secretariat-G eneral Services, Official language, 
Disaster Management, Civil Defence and Fire and Eme rgency Service, Home Guards, etc. 
The Committee presumes that this shortfall in alloc ation is a clear indicator of low 
absorption of funds as highlighted in the trend of expenditure during last financial year and 
hence impresses upon the Ministry to optimize its u tilization capacity to put up a robust case 
for more allocations at RE stage.  
(Para 3.1.10) 
 
CIVIL DEFENCE 
 
 
 The Committee takes serious view of the fact that during 2015-16, there was no 
central financial assistance to the States for revi talization of Civil Defence since all the 
Centrally Sponsored schemes have been subsumed in t he State Plan Funds since 1 
st  April, 
2015. The Committee is not satisfied with the argum ent put forward by the Ministry to 
justify scrapping of central support on the ground of so called higher share of allocation to 
States in the 14 th  Finance Commission.  The Committee is unable to un derstand why the 
Central Government in a federal set up is abdicatin g its responsibilities and leaving the State 
Governments in a lurch.  The Committee is aware of the fact that some State Governments 
are already hard pressed even in terms of paying sa laries to their employees, etc and it is 
almost impossible for them to manage additional res ources to carry out the plan schemes as 
per their own requirement.  Therefore, the Committe e recommends that the Government 
must continue to give financial assistance to the States for revitalisation of Civil Defence.   
(Para 3.2.4) 
FIRE & EMERGENCY SERVICES  
 The Committee takes note of the submission of the Ministry that Fire Services in most 
of the States fall within the jurisdiction of local  bodies which are often starved of financial 
resources hence ill-equipped.  The Committee also t akes note of the submission of the 
Ministry that financial constraints have also been a major factor for the State Governments 
for neglecting Fire Services. The Committee is at p ains to note that the Central Government 
has discontinued Scheme for ‘Modernization of Fire and Emergency Services’ in the name of 
14 th  Finance Commission recommendations which according  to Committee are not 
practicable. The Committee in view of this recommen ds that a serious introspection is 

119 
 
needed by the Central Government in this regard to avoid the problems which might emerge 
due to discontinuation of many centrally sponsored schemes.    
(Para 3.3.5) 
 
RELIEF & REHABILITATION OF MIGRANTS AND REPATRIATES
 
 The Committee is dismayed to note that the Ministr y has been able to spend 
approximately 50% (till 29 th  February, 2016) of allocation made in RE 2015-16 u nder Relief 
& Rehabilitation of Migrants and repatriates(Major Head 3601) as it has utilized only Rs. 
96.24 crore out of allocation of Rs.188.03 crore in  RE 2015-16.  The Committee is not 
satisfied with the submission that Rs. 90 crore is under process for the scheme of 
rehabilitation package for the returnees from forme r Indian enclaves in Bangladesh and 
upgradation of infrastructure in former Bangladeshi  enclaves in India and observes that 
months have passed since enclaves were exchanged wi th Bangladesh but rehabilitation 
package for the Indian enclave dwellers is still no t processed.  Besides, the Committee hopes 
that installment of Rs. 1.76 crore for reimbursemen t to the Government of Tamil Nadu for 
providing relief assistance to the Sri Lankan Tamil refugees will be released soon.  
(Para 3.4.5) 
 The Committee takes serious view of the fact that Rs.174.22 crore only was spent till 
February 2016 out of the allocation of Rs.280 crore  made in RE 2015-16 for relief and 
rehabilitation of Kashmiri migrants under Security Related Expenditure.  The Committee 
would like the Ministry to find out the reasons for  under-utilisation of funds for a scheme 
which calls for immediate attention and take all me asures to hasten the pace of expenditure.  
The Committee would like to be apprised of the detailed action plan drawn in this regard.  
(Para 3.4.9) 
 However, the Committee simultaneously notes that t he Government has approved a 
package in November 2015, involving an estimated ex penditure of Rs.2000 Crore for 
providing financial assistance for additional 3000 state government jobs to the Kashmiri 
migrants and construction of 6000 transit accommoda tions in the Kashmir Valley. The 
Committee hopes that the Ministry would take up wit h the J&K State Government to fast 
pace implementation of this package in terms of pro viding jobs and construction of transit 
accommodation for Kashmiri Migrants.   
(Para 3.4.10) 
 The Committee also notes that the Government has a pproved a proposal with 
estimated expenditure of Rs.13.45 crore per annum, to provide relief to the migrants of the 
hilly areas of Jammu division (who migrated from di fferent hilly areas of Jammu division to 
safer places of nearby towns due to security reasons) at par with the Kashmiri migrants.  The 
Committee appreciates that they will be provided wi th cash relief of Rs.2500 per head per 
month (maximum Rs.10000 per family per month) plus relief for ration/fodder.  The 
Committee is hopeful that this relief measure is im plemented in a time bound manner.  The 

120 
 
Committee would like to be informed about the steps  proposed to be taken to fast track these 
rehabilitation moves of the Government.  
(Para 3.4.11) 
DISASTER MANAGEMENT  
 The Committee is constrained to note that Rs. 919. 29 crore allocated under the Head 
'Disaster Management' in BE 2015-16 was reduced to Rs. 650.99 crore at RE stage due to 
delay in undertaking the works of development of infrastructure of various NDRF battalions.  
The Committee does not want to go into procedural wrangles leading to delay in taking off of 
any project and recommends that procedural complexi ties should be minimized to fast pace 
the project concerning development of infrastructur e of various NDRF battalions.  The 
Committee would like to be apprised of the utilisat ion status of funds in the first quarter of 
the current financial year against the administration sanction order.      
(Para 3.5.10) 
 The Committee takes note of the mandate of World B ank assisted Centrally 
Sponsored Scheme National Cyclone Risk Mitigation P roject (NCRMP) whose objective is to 
address the vulnerability of coastal community to c yclones who are generally poor and are 
from weaker section of the society through minimizi ng the vulnerability to cyclone and 
making people and infrastructure disaster resilient .  The Committee hopes that this project 
will be completed in a time bound manner and go a l ong way in terms of establishing robust 
mitigation infrastructure to with stand cyclone.  T he Committee may be periodically 
apprised of the status of different projects under this scheme and also of the steps being 
taken to avoid any element of delay.  
(Para 3.5.11) 
 The Committee is aware of the fact that the concer ned affected State Governments 
have primary responsibility for disaster management  in the event of occurrence of any 
disaster and the States with their limited resource s undertake relief & rehabilitation 
measures in the wake of natural disasters.  The Com mittee recommends that the Central 
Government should play a major role in the event of  big disasters taking place in any State.   
The Committee also recommends that the Central Gove rnment should set up a monitoring 
mechanism to oversee the status of the rehabilitati on measures taken by the State 
Government without undue interference.   
(Para 3.5.12)

121 
 
comparison to RE 2015-16 under Demand No. 48. Howev er, this nominal increase does not

also notes that the major shortfall has been under Schemes concerning Central Armed Police 
Forces, Security Related Expenditure (SRE), Border Infrastructure & Management and 
Police Infrastructure which are of vital importance  in terms of ensuring internal security in 
the country.  In view of the above, the Committee s trongly recommends that the Ministry of

stage as reduced allocation might hamper ongoing sc hemes/projects as well as taking off of 
new projects.  The Committee impresses upon the Min istry to take steps for judicious and 
optimum utilization of available resources to ward off any impact of decrease in allocation 
vis-à-vis projected allocation. 
(Para 4.1.10) 
NATGRID  
 
 The Committee is aware that NATGRID was conceived after the 2008 Mumbai 
Terror Attack which many in security establishments  believe, could have been averted but 
for delayed scattered intelligence.  It aims to bri ng to one platform intelligence from over 
20 agencies like railways, banks, IB, RAW, CBI, ED,  Central Board of Direct Taxes, etc.  
The NATRGID is expected to use cutting edge technol ogy to place all the details regarding 
telephone, residential, travel and banking of a sus pect on a common platform. However, 
the Committee is extremely disappointed at the slow progress.  NATGRID was conceived in 
2010-11 to be put in place in four phases.  Two pha ses were planned to be completed by 
2014 at an estimated cost of Rs. 1100 crore.  It is disheartening to know that even phase one 
has not been completed till date.  
(Para 4.2.4) 
 The Committee is also aware that construction of a  building to house NATGRID 
was given to NBCC after foundation stone was laid i n 2014 in South Delhi.  It has taken 
around 2 years to get clearance from the State Leve l Environment Impact Assessment 
Authority (SLEIAA), which was granted in November, 2015.  Though as per the Ministry's 
information, construction has begun w.e.f February,  2016,   the estimated cost of the 
proposed complex is Rs. 150 crore but the total bud get allocation in 2016-17 is only Rs. 45 
crore.  It is very clear to the Committee that Gove rnment has gone slow on the NATGRID 
project whereas it strongly feels that his project should be given priority in order to tackle 
high-tech terrorism which has become order of the d ay. Therefore, the Committee 
recommends that the Ministry must prepare a plan wi th clear timeframe within which all 
the four phases should be completed as per set targ et and if required, the Ministry should 
ask for enhanced allocation at RE stage.   
(Para 4.2.5) 
 The Committee is given to understand that NATGRID aims to enhance the country’s 
counter-terrorism capability through sharing inform ation online and providing actionable 
intelligence.  The Committee while noting the reply  of the Ministry, justifying uneven trend 
of allocation of funds meant for NATGRID since last  financial year, hopes that New 
Implementation Plan (NIP) as envisaged would prove to be successful experiment in terms of 
cost effectiveness and conducive to maintain strategic control. Since this project is of national 

122 
 
importance in terms of ensuring internal security o f this country, the Committee also 
recommends that bureaucratic red-tapism should be k ept at minimum to check procedural 
delay without compromising quality in implementatio n of this project.  Besides, the 
Government should make allocation as per requiremen t to help erecting necessary physical 
infrastructure and make NATGRID functional at the earliest.   
(Para 4.2.9) 
DELHI POLICE  
 The Committee observes that there is drastic reduc tion in allocation in B.E. 2016-17 
under plan as well as Non-Plan heads.  The Committe e also notes the details of negative 
consequences which this reduction may cause in term s of paying the salaries to deployed 
forces from outside in Delhi for maintaining law an d order, procurement of latest gadgets, 
weapons and equipments, proper maintenance of build ings of Delhi Police, completion of 
ongoing and new housing/office building projects, p urchase of land for new police 
stations/housing projects.  The Committee understan ds that these are essential components 
to run and modernise any Police Force.  In view of this, the Committee while expressing it 
deep concern over this drastic reduction, recommend s that this should be addressed at RE 
Stage otherwise this reduction would have adverse impact on functioning of Delhi Police.   
(Para 4.3.8) 
 The Committee is not satisfied with the implementa tion status of modernization plans 
of Delhi Police during the last financial year.  Th e Committee finds that in most of the Plan 
schemes such as "Modernization Of Traffic And Commu nication Network Of Delhi Police", 
"Induction Of Latest Technology and Capacity Buildi ng", "Public Private Partnership 
Initiative, the Ministry has not even been able to spend 50% of the allocation made at RE 
2015-16 till 29 th  February, 2016.  It exposes low absorption capacit y of Delhi Police which 
might have led to low allocation by the Finance Min istry in this fiscal.  The Committee 
strongly recommends that Delhi Police should evalua te and analyze the reasons for under 
utilization of funds in its plan initiatives and dr aw an action plan to fast track completion of 
its schemes/projects without further time and cost overruns, otherwise it would be futile 
attempt projecting higher allocation.   
(Para 4.3.9) 
BORDER MANAGEMENT  
 The Committee takes serious view of the fact that only Rs. 3777.40 crore has been 
allocated to Border Management Department against i ts projected demand of Rs. 5045.70 
crore for the financial year 2016-17.  The Committe e feels that the Ministry of Home Affairs 
should reconsider the allocation as the huge deduct ion of around Rs. 1300 crore expenditure 
over border infrastructure and management projects may not be advisable.  Keeping in view 
that the requirement of fencing, flood lighting and  border roads is of utmost importance to 
ensure proper border management and to check extern al security threats, the Committee 
strongly recommends that the Ministry of Home Affai rs should impress upon Finance 
Ministry to further enhance allocation at RE stage.  
(Para 4.5.2) 

123 
 
 The Committee takes note of the status of ongoing as well as new schemes/projects 
likely to be undertaken during this financial year.    The Committee also notes various 
bottlenecks coming in the way of the fencing, flood  lighting and border road projects such as 
issues in land acquisition, bilateral issues, prote sts by people residing in border areas, 
concerns of some border State Governments. The Comm ittee, in view of this, recommends 
that these bottlenecks should be dealt with by taki ng State Governments and people at large 
into confidence.  Issues involving neighboring coun tries who are opposed to fencing, flood 
lighting and border roads in any particular patch o f border areas should be taken up by the 
Ministry of Home Affairs in coordination with the M inistry of External Affairs through 
formal channels.   
(Para 4.5.3) 
 The Committee notes the targets set under phase-II  of the Coastal Security Scheme 
such as construction of Coastal Police Stations, je tties, Marine Operation Centres, boats, 
Rigid Inflatable Boats (RIBs), large vessels, four wheelers and motorcycles.  The Committee 
also notes the submission of the Ministry of Home A ffairs that approval of the Cabinet 
Committee on Security for extension of the period o f implementation of the Phase-II of the 
Scheme for a period of 3 more years, w.e.f. 1 st  April, 2016 is being sought. The Committee 
observes that the phase II of this scheme was suppo sed to be completed in the last financial 
year i.e. 2015-16, however, a lot of work is yet to be done under this scheme.   The Committee 
in view of this while being hopeful that these prop osals would be approved by CCS, strongly 
recommends that the Government should make all out efforts to complete this scheme during 
extended time period without any time and cost overrun. 
  
(Para 4.5.4) 
BORDER AREA DEVELOPMENT PROGRAMME (BADP)  
The Committee notes submission of the Ministry that  Border Area Development 
Programme aims to meet the special developmental ne eds and well being of the people living 
in remote and inaccessible areas situated near the international border through construction 
of physical and social infrastructure.  The Committ ee, however, is constrained to note that 
over the years the fund allocation of the BADP has been grossly inadequate to cater to the 
needs of the border population and the Centre is sh irking its responsibility.   It is also noted 
that the budgetary allocation for BADP for the fina ncial year (2016-17) is Rs. 990 crore 
against projected demand of Rs. 1800.00 crore which  is almost half of the projection.  The 
Committee is of the strong opinion that the develop ment of border areas should not suffer 
for want of funds and therefore recommends that the  allocation may be enhanced at RE 
stage to meet the developmental need of the populac e residing in border areas to check their 
migration from there.   The Committee also recommen ds that the share of BSF under the 
allocation meant for BADP should be increased from mere 10% to at least 40% as they are 
the people who happen to be well versed with the pr oblems faced by populace living in 
border areas and moreover they are much accessible to the people of border areas.     
(Para 4.6.7)

MODERNISATION OF POLICE FORCES (MPF)  
 The Committee notes that Rs. 1753.90 crore has bee n allocated in BE 2016-17 which is 
a steep rise as compared with Rs. 662.11 crore as a llocated in RE 2015-16 under this scheme. 
The Committee also notes submission of the Ministry  that increased allocation is on account 
of consolidation of the some schemes under the Umbr ella Scheme of Modernization of Police 
Forces.  The Committee, however, understands that e ven this increased allocation is not as 
per requirement of the States.  The Committee is of  the view that the State Governments 
require large sum of allocation to modernize their police forces, to improve police 
infrastructure and to launch development initiative s in the Left Wing Extremist affected 
areas.  In view of the above, the Committee recomme nds that big enhancement should be 
made at RE stage and share of Central Government sh ould be increased on all the projects 
under the scheme "Modernisation of Police Forces". Besides, no allocation has been made 
under the Head "Special Infrastructure Scheme for L WE Areas" which is essentially 
required for ensuring development in Left Wing Extr emism affected areas to check growth 
of naxalism and recommends that policy makers shoul d deliberate upon this issue and give 
Special Infrastructure Scheme (SIS) a go ahead.   
 (Para 4.9.8) 
 The Committee further notes that "The Mega City Po licing", a sub-set of the Non-
Plan MPF Scheme was initiated in 2005-06 onwards an d hopes that this scheme will prove to 
be a catalyst in terms of establishing robust polic ing infrastructure and improving safety 
scenario in six identified mega cities.  The Commit tee hopes that the concerned State 
Governments will give priority to this scheme, cont ribute their share and there will be no 
unspent balances lying with the State Governments u nder this scheme.  With the 
implementation of this scheme, the Committee also h opes that policing in the identified mega 
cities will improve to a large extent.  
(Para 4.9.9) 
 The Committee understands that MPF Scheme is an im portant initiative which aims 
at capacity building of the State Police Forces and  help improve police infrastructure in the 
states.  The Committee notes that the utilization p attern of the States is improving over the 
years, however, it recommends that the Ministry sho uld pursue with state Governments 
which are lagging behind, to expedite works under this scheme in their respective States.    
 
(Para 4.9.10)
